Before the conversation could become stranger, another presence entered the edge of the crowd.

Leon.

The murmurs around the announcement wall shifted at once.

Not because people feared him.
Because they were waiting to see whether he would react badly.

To his credit, he did not.
At least not publicly.

He stopped near the panel, read the rankings, and looked at his own line with a controlled expression. Secondary Principal Candidate was still strong placement, especially after his recent losses in image.

Then his gaze moved to mine.

Not angry.
Not openly.

Measured.
Careful.

Leon: "So they really are building around utility."

Cassian glanced at him.

Cassian: "Is that your polite version of surprise?"

Leon ignored him.

Leon: "I admit, Aerlen, I expected the faculty to be less willing to reward unconventional optics."

Aira stared.

Aira: "That may be the most elegant insult I've heard all week."

Haru: "I'm learning to appreciate creativity."

Leon's eyes narrowed a fraction, but the reaction was smaller than it would have been before.

Good.
He was adapting.

Which meant he might eventually become useful.

Or more dangerous.

Often those were the same thing.

Leon looked at the team metrics again.

Leon: "If Group Three remains together, we will need better coordination than what we had in the simulation."

Cassian: "That sounded almost like teamwork."

Leon: "Take a moment to recover from the shock."

Cassian laughed.

Aira blinked.

Aira: "Are we improving or becoming more sarcastic?"

Haru: "Both."

Elira: "That can still be improvement."

For a brief second, all five of us stood there under the floating rankings, surrounded by staring students and fresh rumor energy, and I could almost see the shape of the thing Aurelis was trying to build.

Not harmony.
Not friendship.

Capability.

A team made of unstable parts that might, under enough pressure, become stronger than more elegant combinations.

It was a dangerous theory.

But not an impossible one.

The theory became harder to ignore when we were ordered into a closed debrief instead of released to dinner.

The room assigned to us was one of the smaller tactical classrooms beneath the east wing, all black glass tables, wall-mounted maps, and chairs designed by someone who believed comfort weakened discipline. A crystal recorder floated above the center table, its indicator light glowing a calm blue.

Aira sat down and immediately pointed at it.

Aira: "I want that thing to know I am participating under protest."

The recorder pulsed once, dutifully.

Cassian dropped into the chair opposite her.

Cassian: "It likes you."

Aira: "That is the tragedy of my life."

Leon remained standing for a moment, eyes on the replay slate already forming above the table. The simulation had been rendered into clean blue lines: our route, our mistakes, our corrections, the rescue node, the final extraction.

No cheering.

No rumor.

Just proof.

Elira took the seat beside me. She did not reach for my hand here. Too many crystals. Too many possible logs. But her knee touched mine lightly under the table, a quiet point of contact no recorder would understand.

Lady Seraphine's voice entered through the recorder.

Lady Seraphine: "Group Three will review three failures and three strengths. Begin without faculty prompting. We will assess the conversation afterward."

The light dimmed.

Aira stared at the crystal.

Aira: "They are making us evaluate ourselves."

Leon: "That is standard."

Aira: "No. Standard is an instructor telling us we were disappointing in specific ways. This requires emotional labor."

Cassian leaned forward, suddenly more serious.

Cassian: "First failure. I wanted to break the barrier lock."

Aira blinked.

Aira: "It was... direct."

Cassian: "I am capable of learning when insulted by architecture."

Leon: "You also nearly overcommitted on the rescue node."

Cassian's smile sharpened.

Cassian: "Nearly."

Leon: "Nearly still matters."

For one heartbeat, the old version of Leon showed through: proud, exacting, ready to turn correction into hierarchy.

Then he looked at the replay of himself insisting the false student was only a decoy.

His expression tightened.

Leon: "Second failure. I treated the objective as if it mattered more than the choice around it."

The room went quieter.

No one made a joke.

It was how I knew the sentence had cost him something.

Cassian leaned back, firelight low at his wrist.

Cassian: "We can't save everyone in a real fight."

He said it lightly.

Too lightly.

The kind of lightness people used when a sentence had already cut them once and they had decided jokes were cheaper than bleeding in public.

Elira noticed.

So did Aira.

For once, even Leon did not correct him immediately.

Cassian's thumb moved across the old signet ring on his hand. Red Circle heir jewelry was usually decorative arrogance. His looked worn at the edge, as if someone had turned it too often while waiting for news that arrived too late.

Cassian: "My family calls it triage. Very noble word. Makes leaving someone behind sound like strategy instead of memory."

The fire at his wrist shrank to a thin ember.

Cassian: "I am not fond of that kind of strategy."

Elira answered with the same calm she used in combat.

Elira: "Then we practice saving everyone now, so we know how when it matters."

Elira: "A tournament will try to make us choose between winning and remaining ourselves. We need to decide before the field decides for us."

Aira looked at her.

Aira: "That works as a team principle."

Cassian tapped the table once.

Cassian: "Fine. Principle one: we do not abandon people for cleaner victories."

Leon did not look away from the replay.

Leon: "Agreed."

Aira's eyes softened, but her voice stayed dry.

Aira: "I hate that we are becoming admirable. It raises expectations."

Then the replay shifted to my route calls.

The room became uncomfortable in a different way.

Blue lines traced every warning I had given before visible confirmation. Trap. Decoy. Collapse. Anchor. False core. Real core. Safe path.

Too many correct answers.

Too little visible reason.

Leon watched the sequence twice.

Leon: "Third failure."

I looked at him.

He looked back.

Leon: "We still do not know how to use Haru without exposing him to every question in the room."

It was not the accusation I expected.

It was more dangerous because it was useful.

Aira sat back slowly.

Aira: "That is... annoyingly insightful."

Leon: "Try not to sound shocked."

Cassian grinned.

Cassian: "No, let her have this. I was shocked."

Elira's gaze remained on me.

Elira: "Then principle two: we build explanations before people demand them."

Haru: "You are all assuming this remains our group."

Aira pointed at the replay slate.

Aira: "Haru. Look at that disaster and tell me the faculty will not become attached."

I looked.

Five colored paths crossed, split, collided, corrected, and finally converged into the recovery zone with seventeen seconds remaining.

It was not elegant.

It was alive.

Haru: "They will become attached."

Cassian leaned back, satisfied.

Cassian: "Good. Then principle three: if anyone asks, all our success came from overwhelming charm."

Leon: "Rejected."

Aira: "Violently rejected."

Elira smiled faintly.

Haru: "Recorded."

The crystal above the table pulsed again.

Somewhere, Lady Seraphine was probably writing something severe.

For the first time, I found I did not mind.

---

The next morning confirmed it.

Group Three had been summoned for private advanced training.

That alone was enough to draw attention.

Private training slots were usually reserved for confirmed principal candidates or students whose faculty wanted more direct observation of. In my case, the second possibility felt unpleasantly likely.

We met at dawn in the upper combat terrace where the eastern towers cast long shadows over the practice rings.

The air was cool. The campus quieter than usual. Most students were not yet awake enough to be ambitious.

Professor Halden was already there.
So were Dren and Lady Seraphine.

Aira took one look at the faculty lineup and sighed.

Aira: "I'm beginning to suspect we're not a training group. We're a research incident."

Professor Halden heard her.

Professor Halden: "If that were true, Miss Solen, I would not have informed you."

Aira: "That is not actually comforting."

Cassian stretched his arms over his head.

Cassian: "Can we start? Standing still this early feels unnatural."

Lady Seraphine gestured toward the marked rings.

Lady Seraphine: "Today's focus is role compression."

Leon frowned.

Leon: "Role compression?"

Dren answered.

Dren: "You all performed well when your strengths were allowed to remain distinct. That's useful. It's also fragile. In tournament settings, people get isolated. Plans fail. If one member falls out of position, the whole structure can collapse."

Professor Halden stepped forward.

Professor Halden: "We intend to see whether this team remains functional when deprived of comfort."

Aira: "That is a remarkably hostile way to describe education."

Dren: "Yes."

That ended the discussion.

The drill was simple in concept and terrible in execution.

Every ten minutes, one team member would be removed from their preferred function.

Cassian without front-line pressure.
Aira without stable support position.
Leon without fluid terrain control.
Elira without central anchor authority.
Me without advance reading access.

In other words, the exercise was built to make each of us uncomfortable in different ways.

The first round removed Cassian from direct engagement and forced him into rear interception and protective spacing.

He hated it immediately.

Cassian: "This is insulting."

Aira: "No, this is growth."

Cassian: "I preferred you when you were complaining."

His first interception proved the point against him. Cassian moved too aggressively toward a flank construct, shattered it beautifully, and left the rear lane open for a second attack to slip through.

Aira caught the breach with a barrier that made a sound like offended glass.

Aira: "Growth usually includes not abandoning the thing you are protecting."

Still, he adapted faster than expected. For all his love of impact, he had excellent instinct for threat lines and proved surprisingly good at breaking attacks before they reached the center.

The second round removed Aira from her support grid and forced her into movement leadership.

Aira: "This is malicious."

Elira: "You can do it."

Aira: "Your faith is oppressive."

She did it anyway.
Very well.

The third round took Leon out of structured control and forced him into reactive offense without setup.

That one exposed the most.

Leon was strong. Refined. Skilled.
But much of his confidence depended on environment and rhythm. Without them, he became less certain, less efficient.

He froze for half a breath when the second construct changed angle without warning, frustration flashing across his face because there was no elegant setup to claim.

Aira: "Half-step left, then cut on my count."

He moved on her timing.

It worked.

He noticed that too.
And to his credit, instead of lashing out, he adjusted.

By the fourth rotation, when Elira was barred from acting as central commander, the drill became truly interesting.

Without meaning to, the rest of us had already started orienting around her.

Her presence did that.
Naturally.
Effortlessly.

Removing it created a temporary vacuum.

Cassian wanted motion.
Leon wanted structure.
Aira wanted coherence.

They all looked at me.

I did not enjoy that.

Aira: "Well?"

Haru: "Why are you asking me?"

Aira: "Because you already mapped the next three exchanges in your head."

Cassian grinned.

Cassian: "She's got you there."

Leon looked irritated that the assessment was accurate.

Leon: "If you have a plan, say it."

I looked at the incoming construct pattern.
Three high-pressure lanes. One false center. Delayed flank emergence.

Haru: "I'm not replacing her. I'm holding her position until she's back."

Haru: "Cassian left lane, but don't commit past the second marker. Leon cuts the false center and forces the real flank reveal. Aira, hold mid elevation and feed him angle corrections. Elira, right-side reserve only-don't stabilize unless the outer ring breaks."

They moved.

It worked.

Too well.

The construct wave collapsed almost immediately.

Lady Seraphine made a note.
Professor Halden watched me for a second too long.

I disliked both things.

When the rotation ended, Elira returned to central command without comment. But as we reset positions, she stepped just near enough for only me to hear.

Elira: "You didn't hesitate."

Haru: "There wasn't time."

Elira: "No. I mean with us."

I looked at her.

She smiled very slightly.

Elira: "You trusted we would follow."

It was true.
I hadn't even realized it until she said it.

Haru: "You did."

Elira's gaze softened.

Elira: "Yes."

It was a small exchange.
Quiet.
Easy to miss.

Aira, of course, noticed immediately from across the ring.

Aira: "I would like to complain about whatever private emotional efficiency is happening over there."

Cassian laughed.

Cassian: "So the rumors are true, then?"

Leon, adjusting his sleeves, did not look up.

Leon: "Does it matter?"

Everyone turned to him.

He seemed faintly annoyed by that.

Leon: "I mean strategically."

Aira stared in disbelief.

Aira: "You've become so practical it's unsettling."

Leon: "You say that like I was ever irrational."

Cassian and I answered at the same time.

Cassian: "Yes."

Haru: "Often."

Even Elira laughed at that.

Leon looked actively offended.

Leon: "This team is intolerable."

Aira: "And yet functional."

It was the point, wasn't it?

Functional.
Despite friction.
Despite ego.
Despite rivalry, rumor, class hierarchy, and the fact that one member of the team was secretly a celestial-scale anomaly pretending to be Green.

Aurelis kept pushing us together because it had begun to see the same thing we were beginning to feel.

Not comfort.
Not simplicity.

Potential.

---

Later that afternoon, the second major tournament notice arrived.

This one was public.

Posted in every central corridor, projected over the courtyard, and delivered to candidate groups through official crystal dispatch:

**INTER-NATIONAL SCHOOLS TOURNAMENT - VISITING DELEGATIONS ARRIVE IN 5 DAYS**

At the bottom of the notice, a red student council seal burned with clean authority.

**Emergency movement protocols authorized by Student Council President Rhea Valmont.**

The line was small.

The reaction to it was not.

Two Red Circle students near the fountain stopped arguing mid-sentence and stepped out of the walkway. A cluster of Blue Circle candidates lowered their voices without an instructor telling them to. Even the floating notice crystals adjusted their height to keep the council seal visible above the crowd.

Aira followed my gaze.

Aira: "President Valmont does not usually attach her name to logistics unless she expects people to behave badly."

Cassian gave a short laugh.

Cassian: "Or unless she expects them to survive behaving badly."

Leon frowned.

Leon: "You know her?"

Cassian: "Every Red Circle heir knows Rhea Valmont. Some because they admire her. Most because they have been corrected by her in language their parents could not legally argue with."

I remembered the Red Circle student council president watching me after Leon's first defeat, her smile precise enough to feel like a blade placed flat against glass.

So she had not vanished after noticing the thread.

She had simply been standing where authority stood best.

Slightly ahead of disaster.

Below it appeared the list of confirmed competing institutions and the names of several high-profile student champions already known across the continent.

The campus reaction was immediate.

Excitement rose.
Anxiety sharpened.
Prestige became tangible.

Students crowded the notices, identifying famous names, rumored prodigies, and rival academy legends.

Aurelis' internal selection was no longer theoretical.
Time had become short.

Aira scanned the projected roster with growing dread.

Aira: "This is worse than I thought."

Haru: "You say that often."

Aira: "Because life keeps validating me."

She pointed at the list.

Aira: "Look. Valemere's sending **Soren Ashvale**, the Red Circle duelist with six consecutive academy titles. Drakenshard is sending **Mara Kest**, that battlefield monster everyone keeps politely calling disciplined. And Noctis Dominion is sending..."

She went silent.

That silence did more than the names before it.

Soren Ashvale had earned whispers of admiration.
Mara Kest had earned nervous laughter.

Noctis Dominion earned space.

The students packed around the notice did not all stop talking at once. That would have been too obvious. Instead, conversations thinned in uneven layers. A group of Blue Circle candidates pretended to study Valemere's roster while their eyes kept sliding back to the black Noctis crest. Two Red Circle heirs exchanged looks and said nothing. A Green support student near the edge of the crowd took one step backward without realizing it.

Even the projected banners above the courtyard seemed less bright around Noctis' name.

Not dimmed.

Swallowed.

I had heard of Noctis Dominion College before coming to Aurelis. Everyone had. It was not the kind of school people praised casually. They spoke of its discipline, its victory record, its severe training culture, its graduates who entered military offices, high courts, and private enforcement houses with smiles sharp enough to count as weapons.

Noctis did not produce many beloved heroes.

It produced results.

Leon noticed where my gaze had gone.

Leon: "Their candidates don't break formation."

Cassian's smile faded by one degree.

Cassian: "No. They break other things."

Aira looked at him.

Aira: "It was not comforting."

Cassian: "It was not meant to be."

Elira had not moved.

It was the part that caught me.

Elira Vale could stand before faculty, nobles, duelists, gossip, and pressure without giving away anything she did not choose to reveal. Her composure was not fragile. It was trained, chosen, and reinforced by a will stronger than most magic I had seen at Aurelis.

But now her stillness had changed.

It was not calm.

It was containment.

Whatever name waited after Noctis Dominion, Elira already knew its shape.

And she was holding the memory in place so it would not touch anyone else before it had to.

Elira stepped closer to read.

Her expression changed by half a degree.

It was enough to matter.

Haru: "Who?"

Elira answered quietly.

Elira: "**Lucien Voss.**"

Her fingers closed around the edge of the roster hard enough to crease the projection light. The motion lasted only a heartbeat before she smoothed her hand flat again, composed, public, Yellow Circle perfect.

But I had seen it.

Lucien's name did not frighten her like a rival.

It struck something remembered.

Cassian, standing just behind us, gave a low whistle.

Cassian: "That's a problem."

Leon had approached unnoticed from the side and now looked at the notice with visible tension.

Leon: "Noctis actually sent him?"

Aira looked between them.

Aira: "That is too much collective discomfort for one name. Who is he?"

Elira's gaze remained on the projection.

Elira: "A Yellow Circle."

That alone quieted her.

Elira continued.

Elira: "Noctis Dominion's top prodigy. Lightless affinity variation. Exceptional close-range dominance. He's been called one of the most dangerous young magicians in the tournament circuit."

Cassian folded his arms.

Cassian: "Dangerous is the polite word."

Aira: "And the impolite one?"

Cassian: "Cruel."

Aira looked back at the projection, suddenly less theatrical.

Aira: "Noctis listed him as captain."

The roster unfolded beneath Lucien's name in smaller letters.

**Nera Vail - Blue Circle Binding**
**Oric Sen - Blue Circle Binding**
**Tovan Reiss - Red Circle Close Combat**
**Cael Nox - Conduit Support**

The last entry sat lower than the others, almost like the projection itself had been told to make it less important.

The roster expanded into formation notes when Aira touched the corner of the projection.

Noctis' support pattern unfolded in thin pale lines.

Nera Vail occupied Lucien's right flank. The projection listed water-ice binding, joint containment, route suppression, emergency pressure stabilization. Efficient. Clean. Nothing decorative except the rings shown on both wrists, and even those were probably tools pretending to be jewelry.

Aira leaned closer despite herself.

Aira: "Their support specialist is good."

Leon: "You can tell from a roster?"

Aira: "No. I can tell from what the roster does not bother explaining. See how all the containment paths assume she will adjust after Lucien moves? That is not a weak support caster. That is someone trusted to make his cruelty look like strategy."

Under Nera's name, Oric Sen's lines mirrored hers with less range and more defensive redundancy.

Then Cael's route appeared.

Not beside Lucien.

Through him.

Aira's mouth flattened.

Aira: "That is not a teammate diagram."

Elira's voice stayed quiet.

Elira: "No."

The projection flickered once, showing a practice still from Noctis' qualifying match. Nera stood with one hand extended, blue-gray eyes fixed forward while ice-bindings sealed a hazard corridor. Behind Lucien, Cael's shoulders had locked too high. Nera's gaze had shifted toward him by the smallest fraction.

Then Lucien's hand lifted in the still image.

Nera faced forward again.

Haru: "She saw it."

Aira: "And continued."

Haru: "Yes."

Aira's expression turned sharper, and less amused.

Aira: "I hate mirrors when they are people."

Leon frowned.

Leon: "Conduit support?"

Cassian's expression lost some of its humor.

Cassian: "Noctis likes power routes. A conduit takes strain so the main caster can push harder, longer, or through uglier angles."

Aira: "A terrible job, then."

Elira's voice was quiet.

Elira: "It can be, depending on who is allowed to say no."

The words did not sound theoretical.

Far across the courtyard, in the shadow of the Noctis crest now hovering over the notice board, a smaller projection pulsed and vanished. No one else seemed to notice.

In it, Nera Vail turned toward a pale younger student and said something too clipped for tenderness.

Nera: "Your channels are strained. Report to medical after arrival."

Cael's answer did not reach us.

Nera's did.

Nera: "I am not asking."

Oric Sen stood behind her, expression unreadable. He did not help. He did not stop her either.

The image vanished before Lucien's name brightened again.

Small mercy, I thought, could still be fear wearing a uniform.

A second cold thread joined the first beneath my ribs.

Lucien Voss was no longer only a name attached to Elira's history.

He was a system walking toward us, wearing a student's face.

A breeze passed through the courtyard.
Mana banners shifted overhead.

I watched Elira.
There was something in her face I hadn't seen before.
Not fear.
Not exactly.

History.

Haru: "You know him."

She looked at me then.
Only for a second.
But the answer was already there.

Elira: "A little."

Aira noticed immediately.

Aira: "How little is 'a little'?"

Elira's voice remained composed.

Elira: "Enough."

It was not enough for me.
Not nearly.

But we were in the middle of the courtyard, surrounded by students, faculty, and rumor.
Not the place.
Not yet.

Still, something cold moved quietly under my ribs.

Not jealousy.
Not exactly.

Something simpler.
Protectiveness sharpening into focus.

If this Lucien Voss mattered to Elira's expression even that much, then he mattered to me already.

And I had a feeling the tournament was about to stop being merely competitive.


===== EPISODE 8: The Lightless Name =====

# Episode 8: **The Lightless Name**

The name **Lucien Voss** followed us through the rest of the day.

It did not spread like ordinary gossip.

Ordinary gossip moved quickly because people enjoyed it.

Lucien's name moved carefully because people respected the danger attached to it.

By evening, every candidate in Aurelis knew Noctis Dominion College was sending its top Yellow Circle prodigy. Every hallway conversation carried fragments of the same uneasy facts.

Lightless affinity.

Close-range dominance.

Cruel.

And somehow, beneath all of that, the one detail that mattered most to me remained the one no one else could explain.

Elira knew him.

Not well, she had said.

Enough.

That word stayed with me longer than I wanted it to.

I did not ask her in the courtyard.

That would have been careless. Too many students. Too many faculty eyes. Too much rumor already turning every glance between us into a public event.

So I waited until the academy lights came on and the western garden paths grew quiet.

Elira stood beside me beneath a silver-leaf tree overlooking the lower city. The black necklace rested against her uniform, hidden beneath the appearance of a simple dark crystal pendant. To anyone else, it was pretty. Minorly enchanted. Harmless.

To me, it felt like a vow given shape.

Elira: "You've been quiet."

Haru: "That usually describes me."

Elira looked at me with the gentle patience of someone who had learned the difference between silence and hiding.

Elira: "Not like this."

I exhaled slowly.

Haru: "Lucien."

Her expression changed by less than a breath.

Enough.

Elira turned toward the city lights below the cliff. For a few seconds, she watched the mana bridges glow in the distance as if the answer might be easier to give while looking at something far away.

Elira: "He was part of the junior tournament circuit two years ago."

Haru: "You fought him?"

Elira: "Not officially."

That answer sharpened my attention.

Elira: "Noctis Dominion hosted a youth exhibition. It was supposed to be controlled. Formal. Mostly political theater between schools."

Haru: "And it wasn't."

She shook her head once.

Elira: "Lucien fought a Blue Circle student from Valemere. His opponent was skilled, but clearly outmatched. The match should have ended after the first decisive lock."

Her hand moved unconsciously toward the pendant.

Elira: "Instead, Lucien kept pressing. Not loudly. Not with rage. He was calm. Smiling. He used lightless binding to smother the boy's mana channels one by one until the barrier system registered surrender."

My chest went cold.

Haru: "The officials stopped it?"

Elira: "Late."

No bitterness entered her voice.

That somehow made it worse.

Elira: "The boy lived. He recovered enough to continue school. But his casting never fully returned to what it was."

She did not say the next part immediately.

The wind moved through the silver leaves. Far below, the city lights flickered in ordered constellations, beautiful from a distance and full of people close up. Elira watched them for a moment as if deciding how much of an old ugliness deserved to be carried into this night.

Elira: "Afterward, the official report called it excessive competitive pressure."

Haru: "That is a careful phrase."

Elira: "His name is Talen Mire. His family still writes to ask whether anyone at Aurelis knows a healer who can help him cast at full strength again."

Her fingers closed once against her palm.

Elira: "I have never known what to answer."

Elira: "A dishonest one."

Her voice stayed even. That made the anger beneath it cleaner.

Elira: "I gave a statement. So did two instructors from Valemere. Noctis argued that the surrender barrier had not triggered soon enough, which made continued pressure technically valid."

Haru: "Technically."

Elira: "Yes."

She looked at me then.

Elira: "It was the first time I understood how much cruelty can hide inside rules when the room wants the stronger person to be right."

I thought of Green Circle students corrected into silence. Of Red heirs polished into weapons. Of Yellow prodigies turned into symbols before they were allowed to become people.

Haru: "What happened to Lucien?"

Elira's mouth tightened.

Elira: "Nothing public. Noctis praised his restraint because the boy survived."

The Black Circle gave one slow, cold pulse.

Elira noticed.

Elira: "Haru."

Haru: "I'm listening."

Elira: "I know. Listen to this part too."

She stepped closer, not to soften the story, but to make sure I heard the warning inside it.

Elira: "Lucien does not need everyone to agree with him. He only needs the institution around him to hesitate long enough. He lives in that hesitation."

Briefly, neither of us spoke.

The wind moved through the silver leaves above us. Below the cliff, Aurelis City glittered as if the world had no memory of cruelty.

Haru: "And you intervened."

Elira looked at me then.

Elira: "After the match, yes."

Haru: "What happened?"

Elira: "I told him strength without mercy was just fear wearing a crown."

Despite everything, that sounded exactly like her.

Haru: "And what did he say?"

Her gaze lowered for a moment.

Elira: "That mercy was what powerful people invented to make weakness feel less embarrassing."

The hidden Black Circle beneath my false crest gave one slow pulse.

Not anger.

Not yet.

Recognition.

Haru: "So he hates weakness."

Elira: "No." Her voice softened, but the correction was firm. "He needs weakness to exist below him. There's a difference."

I looked at her carefully.

Haru: "Did he hurt you?"

Elira met my eyes at once.

Elira: "No."

The answer was immediate.

Clean.

True.

Then she stepped a little closer, as if she understood the shape of the question beneath the question.

Elira: "Haru, Lucien was never anything like that to me."

I went still.

Elira: "Not a suitor. Not a lost affection. Not some complicated past that should make you wonder where you stand."

The directness of it caught me more effectively than subtlety would have.

Haru: "You noticed."

Elira's mouth curved faintly.

Elira: "I notice you."

It was unfair.

Not in the way attacks were unfair.

In the way kindness could arrive too accurately and leave no room for defense.

Elira reached for my hand.

Not hidden.

Not dramatic.

Just sure.

Her fingers slipped through mine beneath the silver-leaf tree, warm and steady.

Elira: "You and I are official. Whatever the school says, whatever Lucien assumes, whatever anyone tries to turn this into-there is no question there."

I looked down at our hands.

Then back at her.

Haru: "Official."

The word felt strange.

Not bad.

Just real in a way I had not prepared for.

Elira's expression warmed.

Elira: "Yes."

Haru: "Aira will be relieved."

That startled a laugh out of her.

Elira: "Aira will say she knew before we did."

Haru: "She may be right."

Elira leaned lightly against my shoulder for half a second, so brief it could have been missed by anyone not paying attention.

I was always paying attention to her now.

Elira: "Lucien is dangerous because he enjoys turning people's bonds into pressure points. He watches who you protect, then tries to make protection look like weakness."

Haru: "Then he will misunderstand me."

Elira lifted her head.

Elira: "How?"

I looked toward the dark outline of the tournament banners being raised across the lower courtyard.

Haru: "Protection is not my weakness."

The necklace at her collarbone gave a faint, hidden pulse.

Elira felt it too.

Her fingers tightened around mine.

Elira: "Haru."

Haru: "Yes."

Elira: "Do you?"

There was no accusation in it.

Only concern.

Because she had seen the Black Circle.

She knew that my restraint was not a habit. It was a choice I had to keep making.

Haru: "I won't expose myself over an insult."

Elira: "And if he threatens someone?"

Haru: "Then I will stop him."

Elira held my gaze.

Elira: "Carefully?"

I thought of Leon's spell collapsing in harmless rain.

Of the archive sealed beneath moonlight.

Of the necklace I had made because I could not always be near her.

Haru: "As carefully as the situation allows."

She looked only partly satisfied with that answer.

Which was fair.

It was only partly reassuring.

---

The next morning, Aira stared at our hands for a full five seconds before speaking.

We were standing outside the upper strategy hall, waiting for Group Three's next training session to begin. Students passed in small clusters, slowing at the sight of Elira Vale and the Green Circle transfer standing together with a complete lack of embarrassment.

Aira: "So we're doing this openly now."

Elira: "Good morning, Aira."

Aira: "No. I need the historical record to be accurate."

Haru: "The historical record?"

Aira pointed at our joined hands.

Aira: "Are you two officially dating?"

Several students nearby pretended not to hear and failed with impressive unity.

Elira did not look away.

Elira: "Yes."

I felt half the hallway react.

Aira turned to me.

Aira: "And you?"

Haru: "Also yes."

Aira closed her eyes as if a spiritual burden had finally left her.

Aira: "Good. Finally. My sanity has documentation."

From behind us, Cassian's voice arrived bright with amusement.

Cassian: "I leave you people alone for one evening and diplomacy advances."

Leon followed beside him, expression carefully neutral.

Leon: "It was not subtle before."

Aira looked genuinely wounded.

Aira: "Leon, if you become emotionally perceptive, this team loses balance."

Leon: "I said it was visible. That is not the same thing."

Cassian grinned at me.

Cassian: "Congratulations, Green. You've officially made the academy hierarchy worse."

Haru: "That wasn't the objective."

Cassian: "The best disasters rarely are."

Elira smiled, but her fingers remained comfortably linked with mine.

No denial.

No retreat.

The rumors around us changed shape in real time.

Not vanished.

Changed.

That mattered.

Because secrecy had ruled so much of my life that even this small public truth felt strangely powerful.

Then the strategy hall doors opened.

Professor Halden stood inside.

His gaze lowered briefly to our hands.

Then lifted to my face.

Professor Halden: "How fortunate. Group cohesion appears to be improving."

Aira muttered under her breath.

Aira: "I hate when he says normal things in a suspicious voice."

Professor Halden: "Today's drill will test exactly that."

It would.

---

The new exercise was called **Pressure Disclosure**.

That name alone made Aira object before the rules were finished.

Aira: "Absolutely not. That title sounds like a confession trap with scoring."

Lady Seraphine, standing beside the projection board, did not blink.

Lady Seraphine: "Correct, except the word trap is emotionally imprecise."

Aira: "I withdraw nothing."

The drill placed each team member under simulated isolation, then forced the others to decide whether to continue the objective or break formation to assist. It was not about combat strength alone.

It was about priorities.

Who protected whom.

Who trusted whom.

Who panicked when a bond was stressed.

Halden watched me while Lady Seraphine explained the rules.

I understood then.

This was not only training.

It was observation.

The first two rounds were difficult but manageable. Cassian was isolated behind a collapsing terrain wall and had to rely on Leon's fluid channels to open a route. Leon was pulled into a false duel scenario and forced to accept Aira's support timing instead of controlling everything himself.

Both adapted.

Poorly at first.

Then better.

The third round targeted Elira.

The simulation dimmed.

Not ordinary darkness.

A colorless suppression field spread across the arena floor, swallowing light as it moved. The construct at its center resembled no student exactly, but the resemblance in technique was obvious.

Lightless affinity.

Lucien.

Elira's posture changed.

Only slightly.

Enough that I felt it.

The construct lifted one hand, and pale-black bands snapped toward her from three angles, designed not to injure but to bind her output and force a delayed release.

Her necklace warmed.

I felt it through the vow-thread hidden in the pendant.

Not activation.

Awareness.

The simulation was not a true hostile attack, so the pendant held.

So did I.

Barely.

Aira: "Haru."

Her voice cut across the arena.

She had seen me take one step forward.

Leon saw it too.

Leon: "Hold formation."

That surprised me enough to stop.

Leon did not look at me.

His water mana spread across the floor, mapping the lightless bands with clean, disciplined arcs.

Leon: "If you rush, the field collapses inward. She has the center. We break the anchor points."

It was correct.

Annoyingly correct.

Cassian glanced at me with a sharp grin.

Cassian: "You heard him, Green. Let the Yellow be terrifying for a moment."

Elira's golden light gathered around her hands.

The suppression bands tightened.

For half a second, the old story she had told me lived in the air between us: a boy whose mana channels were smothered one by one while officials reacted too late.

Then Elira moved.

She did not overpower the lightless construct.

She understood it.

Golden threads slid between the pale-black bands, not fighting pressure with pressure but finding the tiny spaces where control had become arrogance. Aira reinforced the openings. Leon cut the outer anchors. Cassian shattered the left stabilizer with one clean blow.

I stayed where I was.

And when the final band tried to close around Elira's throat, I released a pulse so small no one else should have noticed.

Not Black Circle in any visible sense.

Just a correction.

A refusal.

The band missed by an inch.

Elira broke the construct herself.

Golden light burst outward, clean and bright, and the suppression field scattered across the arena like ash.

The drill ended.

Silence followed.

Elira turned toward me.

She knew.

She knew.

But she did not look angry.

Only relieved that I had trusted her enough to wait.

Lady Seraphine made several notes.

Dren looked almost satisfied.

Halden looked fascinated.

It was the least comforting reaction available.

Professor Halden: "Excellent restraint."

I did not answer.

Professor Halden's eyes remained on me.

Professor Halden: "From everyone."

Aira leaned toward Cassian.

Aira: "He added that because he realized it sounded targeted."

Cassian: "It was targeted."

Leon exhaled.

Leon: "Everything here is targeted."

For once, no one argued.

---

The first visiting delegation arrived that evening.

Not the full tournament body.

Only advance representatives, security mages, and early-arrival champions whose schools wanted time to study the arena before the official opening ceremony.

Still, the whole academy felt it.

Students crowded balconies and courtyard rails while the western gate unfolded its ceremonial barrier. Banners from foreign institutions appeared in the air one by one, carried by floating sigils and escort lights.

Valemere Royal Institute arrived first in silver-blue carriages pulled by winged mana constructs.

Drakenshard Military Academy followed with armored transports that looked more like moving fortresses than student vehicles.

Then the air grew quiet.

The Noctis Dominion procession entered without music.

Black glass carriages rolled through the gate under banners marked with a pale eclipse. Their uniforms were dark, formal, and severe, threaded with faint lines of silver. Even their escort lights seemed dimmer than everyone else's.

At the center carriage, the door opened.

Lucien Voss stepped out.

He was tall and composed, with ash-blond hair cut neatly away from a face too calm to be called gentle. His eyes were pale gray, almost colorless, and the Yellow Circle at his collarbone glowed with a strange muted radiance, like sunlight seen through smoke.

The crowd lowered its voice instinctively.

One of the younger Noctis students stepped down behind him too quickly and stumbled when the courtyard's ambient mana pressed against his travel-weary barrier.

It was a small mistake.

Harmless.

Lucien did not turn fully.

He only lifted two fingers.

A pale-black ring closed around the student's wrist and snapped his mana flow into stillness. The boy went rigid, breath catching, while the unstable barrier around him folded into perfect shape.

Lucien: "Posture."

The word was soft.

That made it worse.

The Noctis student bowed his head at once.

Cael's expression did not change, but something behind his eyes went carefully blank-the look of someone who had learned that visible pain only made correction last longer.

Two other Noctis students stepped down behind him without looking at the boy for more than a second.

One was a girl with a narrow braid and blue-gray eyes, her binding rings already aligned around both wrists like jewelry that had forgotten how to be decorative. Nera Vail, if the roster was accurate.

The other, Oric Sen, wore the same Blue Circle insignia, but his gaze measured exits, teachers, and the distance between Lucien and every possible witness.

Tovan Reiss came last, broad-shouldered, red crest burning low and disciplined at his collar. He looked less cruel than Lucien.

That did not make him kind.

It made him someone trained not to ask whether the formation deserved obedience.

The younger student Lucien had corrected kept his eyes down.

Cael Nox.

A conduit support.

Not a teammate in the way Aira was becoming one. Not a rival in the way Leon was struggling to become useful. Not a weapon like Cassian pretended to be when he wanted people to underestimate his judgment.

A route.

It was how Noctis had listed him.

A way for someone else's power to travel.

No anger crossed Lucien's face.

No embarrassment.

No apology.

He had corrected a person the way someone else might straighten a crooked sleeve.

Lucien looked across the courtyard.

Past the banners.

Past the faculty.

Past the staring students.

His gaze found Elira.

Then me beside her.

Then our hands.

A faint smile touched his mouth.

Not warm.

Not surprised.

Interested.

His fingers tapped once against his side.

Only once.

Then they went still again.

Lucien walked toward us with the patient confidence of someone used to rooms making space before he asked.

Elira did not move behind me.

I did not step in front of her.

We stood side by side.

That mattered too.

Lucien stopped a few paces away and gave a polished bow just shallow enough to be insulting if someone cared to notice.

Lucien: "Lady Elira. Aurelis looks brighter than I remember."

Elira: "Lucien."

No honorific.

No warmth.

His eyes moved to me.

Lucien: "And this is?"

Before anyone else could answer, Elira's hand tightened around mine.

Elira: "Haru Aerlen. My partner."

The courtyard heard it.

All of it.

Aira, standing behind us, whispered with something like reverent exhaustion.

Aira: "There. Official enough for architecture."

Lucien's smile did not change, but something behind his eyes sharpened.

Lucien: "A Green Circle partner for Aurelis' golden prodigy."

For less than a heartbeat, the polish cracked.

Not anger.

Surprise.

As if he had expected Elira to deny the rumor, laugh it aside, or dress it in political language sensible people could file away.

As if a Yellow Circle prodigy genuinely choosing a Green Circle student was something his world had no shelf for.

Then the mask returned.

Cold.

Precise.

Contemptuous.

But I had seen it.

Murmurs moved through the crowd.

He had not raised his voice.

He did not need to.

The title did the work for him. Green Circle. Partner. Golden prodigy. A neat little arrangement of words designed to make every watching student decide whether I was unworthy, whether Elira was careless, or whether Aurelis had begun hiding strategy behind romance.

Lucien let the murmurs breathe.

Then he looked at our joined hands again.

Not possessive.

Appraising.

He looked at them the way a collector might look at a priceless blade being used to cut rope.

Lucien: "How generous of Aurelis."

Elira's expression did not change.

Lucien: "Most schools would assign a Green Circle escort to logistics, not reputation."

The words passed through the courtyard like a wire pulled tight.

Several students laughed before they decided whether it was safe to laugh.

Then they stopped, because Elira had turned her head.

Not quickly.

Not dramatically.

Just enough for the nearest laugh to die in the student's throat.

I felt Elira's mana warm at my side.

Not defensive.

Steady.

I answered before she needed to.

Haru: "Haru is fine."

Lucien looked at me directly for the first time.

His gaze dropped to my crest.

Green.

Small.

The easiest lie in the world for someone like him to believe.

Lucien: "Is it?"

He sounded genuinely curious, which made the insult sharper.

Lucien: "Forgive me. I am trying to understand the local custom. At Noctis, Yellow Circle prodigies are not encouraged to dilute their standing for sentimental experiments."

Aira inhaled behind us.

Cassian's fire crest gave one amused, dangerous flicker.

Leon went very still.

Elira's hand remained in mine.

Her fingers did not tighten this time.

They relaxed.

That was worse for Lucien.

Elira: "Then Noctis remains educationally limited."

Someone in the crowd made a sound that was almost a laugh and almost fear.

Lucien's smile became finer.

Lucien: "I see. So it is not a strategy."

Elira: "No."

Lucien: "Not a cover."

Elira: "No."

Lucien: "Not a charity."

For the first time, her gold crest brightened.

Only at the edge.

Enough.

Elira: "Careful."

Lucien's eyes flicked to the crest, then back to her face.

Lucien: "There she is."

The line was soft, satisfied, and cruel in the precise way of someone who enjoyed making dignity defend itself.

For a heartbeat, his lightless mana brushed the air around my false Green Circle crest.

Testing.

Measuring.

Dismissive.

Then the edge of that pale mana drifted too near Elira's pendant.

The necklace did not glow.

It did not reveal itself.

It simply became, for the smallest instant, utterly impossible to touch.

Lucien's eyes narrowed by a fraction.

The Black Circle beneath the camouflage pressed cold behind my sternum.

Ozone and metal touched the back of my tongue.

I kept it asleep.

Lucien: "Interesting."

Professor Halden stood near the faculty line, watching the exchange with far too much attention.

He was.

Lucien inclined his head toward Elira again.

Lucien: "I look forward to seeing whether Aurelis has grown stronger, or merely more sentimental."

Elira's voice remained calm.

Elira: "It has."

Lucien's gaze flicked once more to me.

Lucien: "Then I look forward to seeing whether Aurelis protects its choices after the audience loses patience with the performance."

He turned and walked toward the Noctis delegation without waiting for dismissal.

Conversations restarted badly, in uneven pieces, as if everyone had to decide which version of the scene they wanted to repeat later.

Aira stepped up beside us.

Aira: "I dislike him immediately."

Her voice was light.

Her pencil had snapped in her hand.

She looked down at the two pieces as if personally offended by them.

Aira: "And now I need a new pencil, which makes it political."

Leon, on her other side, looked unusually serious.

Leon: "It was him being polite."

His gaze stayed on the place where Lucien had stood.

For once, there was no old rivalry in the way he looked at our joined hands.

Only the uncomfortable recognition of a room being invited to laugh at rank.

Cassian smiled, but there was no humor in it.

Cassian: "Good. I was worried the tournament might be boring."

Elira looked at me.

Her hand was still in mine.

Haru: "I'm all right."

Elira: "I didn't ask."

Haru: "You were going to."

The corner of her mouth softened despite the tension.

Elira: "Maybe."

Across the courtyard, Lucien paused beside the Noctis banners and looked back once.

Not at Elira this time.

At me.

Elira watched him go.

Elira: "He was surprised."

Aira frowned.

Aira: "That was surprise?"

Elira: "And contempt."

Her hand tightened once around mine, then steadied again.

Elira: "Lucien believes in the Circle hierarchy the way some people believe in gravity. Not as policy. As truth."

Haru: "And we violated his truth."

Elira: "Yes."

Cassian's grin faded.

Cassian: "That sounds worse than irritating him."

Elira: "It is. Irritation passes. Lucien corrects violations."

Leon looked toward the black banners.

Leon: "So next time he will be prepared."

Elira: "Yes."

Across the courtyard, the Noctis formation closed around Lucien again.

Cael Nox stood exactly where a conduit support was supposed to stand: close enough to be useful, far enough back that no one important had to look at him unless something went wrong. He flexed the wrist Lucien had corrected, then stopped when Nera Vail's gaze touched the motion.

She saw the tremor.

So did Oric.

Neither of them moved.

Not because they did not understand.

Because Noctis had trained understanding to arrive after permission.

Lucien spoke without turning.

Lucien: "Cael."

The boy's hand dropped to his side.

Cael: "Yes, Voss."

Lucien: "Tomorrow's calibration will use the extended relay pattern."

Nera's binding rings clicked once.

Too softly for most of the courtyard to hear.

Oric heard it.

Cael heard it.

Lucien heard it too.

His pale eyes shifted toward Nera with the mild interest of a teacher noticing poor posture.

Lucien: "Is there a technical concern?"

Nera's expression stayed composed.

Nera: "Extended relay patterns increase conduit strain if the anchor overcorrects."

Lucien smiled.

Lucien: "Then the conduit should not overcorrect."

Cael's face emptied itself of reaction.

That was what made the moment stay with me.

Fear had many shapes. Panic was the easiest to notice. Obedience was quieter and far more useful to cruel people.

Nera lowered her eyes first.

Nera: "Understood."

Oric's mouth opened by a fraction, then closed.

Lucien turned away as if the conversation had been about equipment.

The Noctis formation moved on.

But Nera looked back once.

Not at me.

At Cael's wrist.

Then at her own rings.

For the first time since Lucien stepped through the gate, her discipline looked less like loyalty than a lock waiting for someone to admit it existed.

Professor Halden did not return directly to the faculty residences after the delegations dispersed.

He went to the sealed records annex beneath the advanced mana studies wing and opened a drawer that had not been indexed in the public catalog for forty years.

He had watched the courtyard exchange from the faculty line.

Lucien Voss's reaction had been instructive.

Not the insult. Insults were easy.

The surprise.

Genuine, quickly buried, and therefore more useful than anything Lucien had chosen to say aloud.

Elira Vale had publicly chosen a Green Circle partner, and Lucien Voss had needed a heartbeat to make the fact fit inside his worldview.

Halden disliked heartbeats that changed investigations.

The file inside was thin.

Too thin for a myth that had frightened entire institutions.

Black Circle references did not survive in clean histories. They survived in warnings, redactions, disaster summaries, and committee reports written by people trying to sound calm after doing something unforgivable.

Halden read until the mana lamp beside him burned low.

One fragment described a city-state that tried to conscript an unclassified celestial-rank caster. Another described a royal college that attempted containment under faculty authority. A third ended halfway through a sentence, the remaining page burned into glass.

He was not hostile to Haru Aerlen.

That would have been simpler.

He was terrified of what history taught: every time something like Black Circle emerged, institutions fell. Not because the power was evil, necessarily, but because people could not resist trying to control it, weaponize it, or destroy it. And in trying, they destroyed everything else.

Halden closed the file with two fingers.

Lucien would not remain surprised.

That was the problem with intelligent cruelty.

It learned.

Professor Halden: "Document first."

His voice sounded too loud in the archive.

Professor Halden: "Possess nothing."

Curiosity remained.

So did fear.

For once, he allowed both to stand without pretending one was a virtue and the other was weakness.

The hidden darkness near my heart pulsed slowly.

Not restless.

Patient.

The visiting schools had begun to arrive.

The tournament had not officially started.

But the first challenge had already been made.

Near the Valemere carriages, Soren Ashvale watched the Noctis delegation disappear beneath the black banners.

One of his teammates, a silver-blue support caster with her arms folded tightly, leaned closer.

Valemere Student: "Voss does not waste much time."

Soren's expression remained polite in the way duelists sometimes looked polite before drawing a blade.

Soren: "Voss does not compete."

His teammate looked at him.

Soren: "He dominates. There is a difference."

Across the courtyard, Elira still stood beside me with her hand in mine, unhidden despite every whisper Lucien had tried to sharpen.

Soren saw that too.

He looked away first.

Not bitterly.

Respectfully.

Valemere Student: "Will Valemere get involved?"

Soren adjusted the worn dueling glove over one scarred hand.

Soren: "If this becomes about honor, yes."

Then he turned back toward his own delegation, leaving the sentence behind like a promise that had not yet chosen its battlefield.


===== EPISODE 9: The Opening Fault =====

# Episode 9: **The Opening Fault**

The tournament began with bells.

Not academy bells.

War bells dressed as ceremony.

At sunrise, every tower of Aurelis Arcane Academy rang in layered silver tones that moved through the campus like waves across glass. The sound reached the dormitories, the training fields, the guest wings, the city below the cliffs, and every student pretending they had slept well.

I had not slept much.

It was not unusual.

What was unusual was the reason.

Lucien Voss had looked at me once across the courtyard, and the hidden Black Circle beneath my false crest had answered with patience instead of sleep.

That bothered me.

Power did not usually react to people.

Threats, yes.

Systems, yes.

Ancient seals, collapsing barriers, unstable spells, things that carried enough force to matter.

But Lucien was not powerful enough to threaten what I truly was.

Not even close.

So the reaction was not fear.

It was recognition.

As if something in his lightless mana belonged to an old language my power remembered before I did.

I stood before the mirror in my dormitory room and adjusted my uniform collar until the false Green Circle crest rested harmlessly where everyone expected it to be.

Small.

Dim.

Acceptable.

Haru Aerlen, Green Circle transfer student.

Not the boy whose real magic had stirred because a Noctis prodigy smiled too calmly at the person I loved.

A knock came at my door.

I opened it.

Aira stood in the hallway with a folded tournament schedule, two nutrient bars, and the expression of someone who had already fought three disasters before breakfast.

Aira: "Eat one."

Haru: "Good morning."

Aira: "That is aspirational. Eat."

I accepted the bar.

Aira watched until I opened it.

Aira: "Elira is waiting near the east stairs. Leon is pretending not to be nervous. Cassian is pretending nervousness is a breakfast seasoning. The opening ceremony begins in forty minutes, and Professor Halden has already looked satisfied twice."

Haru: "That last one seems serious."

Aira: "It is the worst item on the list."

We started walking.

The dormitory corridors were already full of tournament candidates in polished uniforms. Aurelis students wore silver-blue accents. Visiting students wore their own colors: Valemere's white and blue, Drakenshard's iron red, Sol Aeternum's bright sun-gold, Noctis Dominion's severe black and silver.

The academy no longer felt like a school.

It felt like a stage.

And everyone on it understood that losing would be remembered.

Elira waited near the east staircase with sunlight falling behind her. The black necklace rested beneath the appearance of a dark crystal pendant at her collarbone. Her Yellow Circle crest glowed above it, steady and bright.

She smiled when she saw me.

Not for the school.

For me.

That helped.

Elira: "You look awake."

Haru: "That is kind."

Aira: "It is inaccurate."

Elira's smile warmed, but her eyes studied my face carefully.

Elira: "Lucien?"

There was no need to ask what she meant.

Haru: "Partly."

Her hand brushed mine for one quiet second. Not hidden, not dramatic. Just enough.

Elira: "We face what is in front of us. Not what he wants us to imagine."

Haru: "That is strategy."

Elira: "It is also self-care."

Aira looked between us.

Aira: "I dislike how reasonable romance is making both of you."

Leon joined us at the bottom of the stairs, adjusting one glove with unnecessary precision.

Leon: "Are we ready?"

Aira: "You asked that like a normal teammate. I am unsettled."

Leon: "I can insult someone if that restores your balance."

Cassian arrived behind him with his red hair tied back and his formal mantle hanging open as if protocol had personally offended him.

Cassian: "Save it for Noctis. They look like funeral statues with excellent posture."

Leon: "Do not start an international incident before the first event."

Cassian: "I said excellent posture. It was diplomacy."

Elira: "It was almost diplomacy."

The five of us walked toward the Grand Arena together.

Group Three.

A Yellow Circle prodigy.

A hidden Black Circle.

A tactical Blue who worried professionally.

A prideful Blue learning teamwork against his will.

A Red Circle heir who smiled at danger like it owed him money.

It should not have worked.

It was probably why Aurelis was interested in us.

---

The Grand Arena floated above the central courtyard on six enormous mana pylons.

During normal school weeks, it was dormant.

Today it had unfolded into a tournament coliseum large enough to hold the academy, the visiting delegations, city officials, sponsors, judges, faculty, and thousands of projected spectators watching from remote viewing halls across the continent.

Tiered seats curved around a massive central field of white stone and black glass. Floating screens displayed school crests. Barrier rings turned slowly overhead like transparent halos. At the highest platform, the judges sat beneath the continental tournament banner.

The Inter-National Schools Tournament had officially arrived.

Aurelis students cheered when our delegation entered.

Valemere answered with polished applause.

Drakenshard struck their fists against armored forearms in a rolling metallic rhythm.

Sol Aeternum released harmless golden sparks into the air.

Noctis Dominion made no sound at all.

That silence drew more attention than cheering would have.

Lucien stood at the front of their delegation, hands folded behind his back, expression calm enough to look bored.

His gaze found Elira first.

Then me.

Then our team.

He smiled faintly.

Aira: "I want it recorded that I do not like being smiled at like a research sample."

Haru: "Recorded."

Cassian: "I feel flattered."

Leon: "That is because your survival instincts are decorative."

Before Cassian could answer, the arena lights dimmed.

Headmaster Veris stepped onto the central platform.

His voice carried through amplification runes.

Headmaster Veris: "Students, faculty, honored guests, and representatives of the participating nations. Aurelis Arcane Academy welcomes you to the Inter-National Schools Tournament."

Applause rolled through the arena.

He continued with the formal history.

Prestige.

Excellence.

The responsibility of power.

The usual language institutions used when they wanted competition to sound noble.

I listened with half my attention.

The other half watched the barrier rings.

Something in the upper eastern array was misaligned by less than a finger's width.

Too small for most people to notice.

Too deliberate to be maintenance error.

I looked toward the faculty platform.

Professor Halden was already watching me.

He was.

Beside him, Lady Seraphine's eyes moved from the barrier ring to my face, then back to the field.

She had noticed something too.

Not enough.

But something.

The Headmaster raised one hand.

Headmaster Veris: "This year's opening event will be the **Crest Relay Trial**."

The central field transformed.

White stone sank away. Black glass split into five paths. Forest, ruins, water channels, floating bridges, and mirrored corridors rose from the floor in a structured simulation.

A display appeared overhead.

**OPENING EVENT: CREST RELAY TRIAL**

**Objective:** Each school sends one five-person team.

**Goal:** Secure three crest fragments and return them to the center gate.

**Scoring:** Time, teamwork, mana control, rescue response, and penalty avoidance.

**Combat:** Limited interference permitted. Direct injury prohibited.

**Warning:** The field changes every five minutes.

Aira stared at the rules.

Aira: "Rescue response is scored?"

Leon: "That means the field will create rescue scenarios."

Aira: "I understand. I object to being emotionally prepared."

Cassian rolled his shoulders.

Cassian: "Limited interference permitted. Good."

Elira: "Direct injury prohibited."

Cassian: "Also good. Less paperwork."

Professor Halden approached our team from the side of the platform.

Professor Halden: "Group Three will represent Aurelis in the opening event."

Leon went still.

Aira: "Immediately? We are doing this immediately?"

Professor Halden: "The tournament rarely improves when students are given time to overthink."

Aira: "I have built my identity around overthinking."

Professor Halden ignored that.

His gaze settled on me.

Professor Halden: "Aerlen, your role remains tactical movement analysis."

Haru: "Understood."

Professor Halden: "Try to be appropriately impressive."

Haru: "That seems subjective."

His mouth moved almost into a smile.

Professor Halden: "Precisely."

Elira's shoulder shifted closer to mine.

Just enough for me to feel the warning.

Not here.

I knew.

On the opposite platform, Noctis Dominion's team stepped forward.

Five students.

Lucien at the center.

Two dark-uniformed Blue Circle binders.

A Red Circle close-quarters striker.

And the younger student Lucien had corrected the previous evening, face pale but posture perfect.

Lucien looked across the distance.

Lucien: "Good luck, Lady Elira."

Elira: "To your team as well."

His gaze slid to me.

Lucien: "And to your Green."

The arena heard the phrase.

It was polite enough to pass.

Possessive enough to cut.

Elira did not move.

Her voice stayed calm.

Elira: "Haru is not mine because he is weak."

The murmurs began instantly.

Elira: "He is beside me because I trust him."

The murmurs became silence.

I looked at her.

She did not look away from Lucien.

Lucien's smile thinned.

Haru: "We should begin before the opening ceremony becomes philosophical."

Aira: "Too late."

The starting sigils lit beneath our feet.

The field waited.

The timer appeared overhead.

**CREST RELAY BEGINS IN 00:10**

Ten seconds.

Elira's voice lowered.

Elira: "Roles."

Leon answered first.

Leon: "Cassian and I take forward pressure. Aira handles route stability. Elira anchors defense and high-output response. Haru calls changes."

No hesitation.

No insult.

Progress, then.

Cassian grinned.

Cassian: "Look at us, blades mostly pointed outward."

Aira: "Do not name it. You'll scare it."

The timer struck zero.

We moved.

---

The first section was forest.

Not decorative forest.

Hostile forest.

Trees shifted when we passed. Roots tried to catch ankles. Silver birds made of mana dove from branches and shattered into blinding flashes if struck too hard.

Across the field, the other schools entered their own mirrored sections. The paths were separate at first, but the rules allowed intersections later.

I watched the terrain breathe.

Haru: "Left roots respond to heat. Cassian, do not burn them. Leon, water pressure under the second ridge. Aira, the birds are triggered by sudden mana spikes."

Cassian: "Do not burn the roots. Cruel opening."

Leon sent a thin water line beneath the ridge. The roots lifted harmlessly before we reached them.

Aira shaped a low mist screen that softened the flash-birds' detection. They tilted in the branches, confused, while we passed under them.

Elira walked at the center, golden light held close instead of bright.

Restraint from a Yellow Circle looked different from restraint from me.

Hers looked like grace.

Mine looked like lying.

The first crest fragment appeared in a hollow of black stone.

Too easy.

Haru: "Stop."

Everyone stopped.

Even Cassian.

It was progress too.

The hollow shimmered.

Aira narrowed her eyes.

Aira: "False floor."

Leon crouched.

Leon: "Backlash net under it. If we grab the crest directly, it marks a penalty."

Cassian: "Can we break it?"

Aira and Leon answered together.

Aira: "No."

Leon: "No."

Cassian sighed.

Cassian: "The tournament hates clean targets."

Elira lifted one hand. Golden threads extended into the hollow, not touching the crest, only measuring the light around it.

Elira: "It wants simultaneous stabilization from three points."

Aira: "I can take left."

Leon: "Right."

Haru: "The third point is not in the hollow."

They looked at me.

Leon frowned.

Leon: "The hollow signature is cleaner."

Haru: "Too clean. Real mana under this much distortion should have micro-fluctuations."

That sounded like something a careful Green Circle support student might notice.

It was not the whole truth.

I pointed at a tree behind us.

Haru: "It is using the shadow as an anchor."

Briefly, nobody spoke.

Cassian: "That is irritatingly clever."

Elira smiled slightly.

Elira: "Good catch."

I kept my face neutral.

Praise was harder to defend against than suspicion.

We solved the lock in nine seconds.

The crest fragment rose into Elira's hand, glowing silver-blue.

**AURELIS: FRAGMENT ONE SECURED**

Cheers erupted from our side of the arena.

Across the field, Drakenshard secured theirs through brute coordination, shattering half their trees and taking only one penalty.

Valemere was slower but clean.

Sol Aeternum moved beautifully.

Noctis was fastest.

Lucien did not seem to hurry.

It was the worst part.

His team moved as if the field had already been explained to them by someone who disliked surprises.

Nera and Oric did not waste motion binding the forest hazards. They touched only the joints that mattered, letting roots freeze half-grown and flash-birds fall asleep mid-dive. Tovan cleared the larger threats with short red strikes that never exceeded the minimum force required.

Cael followed three steps behind Lucien with both hands held near his chest, lightless lines running from his wrists into the ground whenever Lucien lifted a finger.

Conduit support.

The phrase looked worse in motion.

Cael was not being harmed. Not visibly. The tournament barriers would have reacted if his channels crossed injury thresholds.

But he was being used constantly, quietly, efficiently.

Lucien did not turn to check whether the strain hurt.

He only assumed the route would carry him.

Aira saw it too.

Aira: "I hate that roster title more now."

Leon followed her gaze and said nothing.

His silence had weight in it.

---

At the five-minute mark, the field changed.

The forest folded into ruins.

Walls rose. Bridges collapsed. Mirrors appeared between stone arches, showing false versions of teammates moving in the wrong directions.

Aira groaned.

Aira: "Illusion corridors. Wonderful. I was worried the building did not hate us personally."

Leon: "Stay on formation."

Cassian: "Which formation?"

Haru: "The one where you do not punch yourself in a mirror."

Cassian glanced at me.

Cassian: "It was one time in practice."

Aira: "It was this morning."

The second crest fragment flashed at the far end of a ruined hall.

Between us and it stood Valemere's team.

Not hostile.

Not friendly.

In the center of their formation was Soren Ashvale, the Red Circle duelist Aira had mentioned before. He had dark hair, a neat uniform, and the posture of someone raised to treat pressure as manners.

Soren: "Aurelis."

Elira: "Valemere."

Soren's gaze flicked from Elira to me, then to our fragment.

Soren: "Temporary passage conflict."

Leon: "There are two routes."

Soren: "One is trapped."

Aira: "Both are trapped."

Soren looked at her.

Aira: "What? They are."

The ruins shifted again.

Behind Valemere, a Noctis lightless thread slipped along the floor.

Thin.

Almost invisible.

It was not aimed at us.

It was aimed at the bridge beneath Valemere's rear support student.

If it cut the support line, the bridge would collapse. Valemere would lose time, possibly take injury, and Noctis would gain advantage while remaining technically uninvolved.

Lucien was across the field, not looking at us.

He was not looking.

I had choices.

Warn Valemere and reveal that I saw what I should not see.

Do nothing and let the trap spring.

Intervene subtly and risk Halden seeing another impossible correction.

Elira saw my hand twitch.

She did not stop me.

She only moved half a step to cover the angle from the judges.

Trust, given shape.

I released a tiny pulse through the floor.

Not power.

Timing.

The lightless thread struck the bridge support and found it already vibrating at the wrong frequency. Instead of cutting through, it slid aside like a blade hitting oil.

The bridge held.

Soren's rear support student stumbled but did not fall.

Soren turned sharply.

His eyes narrowed at the floor.

He had felt enough to know something had happened.

Not enough to know what.

Lucien's gaze moved toward us at last.

There it was.

Interest sharpening.

Aira whispered without moving her lips.

Aira: "Please tell me that was legal."

Haru: "Technically."

Leon: "That is never comforting from you."

Soren looked at Elira.

Soren: "We owe Aurelis a clean passage."

Elira: "Then take the right route. The left collapses in twelve seconds."

Soren's gaze moved once toward Noctis, where Lucien had still not looked back at the student his thread had nearly dropped.

The warmth left Soren's eyes.

Soren: "It was not dueling."

One of his Valemere teammates stiffened.

Valemere Student: "Should we report it?"

Soren's hand closed once, scarred knuckles whitening under the glove.

Soren: "Aurelis saw it. If they need witnesses, Valemere will stand."

He believed her immediately.

Valemere moved right.

We moved left.

Cassian laughed under his breath.

Cassian: "I enjoy diplomacy when it has traps."

We reached the second crest fragment at the same time Noctis reached theirs.

Lucien lifted his fragment from a mirror basin without touching the water.

I watched the basin ripple backward.

The field did not like him.

New information shifted the room.

**AURELIS: FRAGMENT TWO SECURED**

**NOCTIS: FRAGMENT TWO SECURED**

The crowd roared.

The visible scoreboard called it equal progress.

The field did not.

Beneath the cheering, the terrain gave one subtle shudder under Noctis' path. Not a penalty. Not an alarm. More like a living thing flinching after being handled too neatly. The tournament system recorded outcomes; the old academy infrastructure remembered pressure.

Professor Halden's head turned toward the lower field array.

Lucien's did not.

That meant he either had not noticed, or had expected it.

Neither option improved the situation.

Cael stumbled once as the Noctis fragment locked into place.

It was so brief most of the arena missed it.

Nera did not. She reached halfway toward him, then stopped when Lucien's shoulder shifted by a fraction.

Not a command.

Enough of one.

Elira saw that too.

Her hand tightened around the fragment we carried.

Elira: "He trains hesitation into them."

Haru: "Yes."

Across the field, Nera's binding rings settled back against her wrists.

Her face returned to Noctis discipline before the judges could read anything from it, but not before Aira caught the flicker.

Aira: "She wanted to help him."

Leon: "Then why didn't she?"

Nera stepped back into formation. Oric moved half a pace closer to Cael, just enough that the younger student's next stumble would look like a planned adjustment if anyone asked.

Aira's eyes stayed on them.

Aira: "Because in Noctis, wanting is not the same as being allowed."

Haru: "That is a dangerous difference."

Aira: "It is also a useful one."

Leon looked across the field, jaw tense.

Leon: "Then we do the opposite."

Aira stared at him.

Aira: "It was a very decent sentence, and I need a moment."

Leon: "Take it while moving."

For the first time, the opening event felt less like a trial.

It felt like a race.

---

The third field change was water.

The ruins broke apart beneath our feet and became floating platforms over a black lake. Bridges appeared and vanished. Mana currents twisted under the surface. The final crest fragment hovered above the center gate, but every school now shared the same zone.

Limited interference was permitted.

That meant everyone became a problem.

Drakenshard charged first.

Their front-line student, Mara Kest, crossed three unstable platforms with raw reinforcement and no visible concern for physics. She swung a blunt training blade into a barrier pylon, forcing the gate angle to shift toward her team.

Cassian's eyes lit up.

Cassian: "I like her."

Aira: "You like everyone who creates property damage."

Leon: "Focus."

Noctis moved next.

Lucien did not chase the fragment.

He chased the field.

His lightless mana sank into the lake and dimmed the currents around the center gate. Platforms began moving out of rhythm. Not broken. Not illegal. Just inconvenient for everyone who did not understand what he had changed.

Elira's expression cooled.

Elira: "He is forcing rescue penalties."

Her jaw tightened.

She had seen this before. He was using the same suppression logic from the junior circuit, refined until cruelty fit inside tournament legality.

Haru: "Yes."

Aira: "Because if people fall, teams lose time helping them."

Leon: "Or lose points if they don't."

Cassian's smile disappeared.

Cassian: "That is ugly."

Haru: "It is efficient."

Elira looked at me.

Haru: "I did not say I approved."

The first victim was a Sol Aeternum student.

Her platform slid sideways under her foot. She caught herself with a burst of golden fire, but the flame triggered a water inversion. The lake pulled up like a hand and dragged her toward the surface.

Her team moved too slowly.

Lucien watched.

Not smiling.

That made it worse.

Leon moved first.

He did not ask.

He snapped both hands outward, sending twin lines of water mana across the lake. They wrapped around the Sol student and pulled her sideways, away from the inversion field.

It cost us speed.

It saved her from a penalty and possible injury.

The judges' display flashed.

**AURELIS: RESCUE RESPONSE CREDIT**

Across the water, the Sol Aeternum student caught the edge of her team's platform and looked back at him.

Sol Student: "Thank you."

Leon froze for half a second, as if surprised the system had rewarded him for not being selfish.

Aira: "Good choice."

Leon: "I know."

Aira: "You are allowed to just accept praise."

Leon: "Not during an event."

Cassian launched forward before another platform vanished.

He struck the air with a controlled fire burst, not to attack, but to create upward pressure that stabilized the path for Elira.

Elira crossed it instantly.

Golden light spread from her feet in thin rings, reading the lake.

Lucien noticed.

For the first time, he moved directly toward her.

The arena seemed to feel it.

Two Yellow Circles approaching the same center gate.

Elira's light, warm and precise.

Lucien's lightless field, pale and cold.

The final crest fragment hovered between them.

Lucien: "Still trying to save everyone?"

Elira: "Still mistaking cruelty for clarity?"

He tilted his head.

Lucien: "Clarity does not require kindness."

Elira: "No. But strength does."

Their mana met.

The center gate shook.

Not from force.

From contradiction.

Golden threads pushed through lightless rings. Pale bands tried to close around the fragment. Elira did not flinch. She shifted one foot, drew his binding pattern wide, and left space behind it.

For me.

Haru: "Aira, third current under Elira's left. Leon, cut the lake pull. Cassian, prepare to break the right pylon but not yet."

Aira: "That is a lot of instructions."

Haru: "Yes."

Aira: "I hate that they make sense."

She moved.

Leon moved.

Cassian waited, which was the most impressive part.

Lucien's field tightened.

The necklace at Elira's throat warmed.

Not activating.

Warning.

Lucien's pale mana slid toward the pendant again, curious and invasive.

I stepped onto the edge of the platform.

Elira did not look back.

Elira: "Haru."

One word.

Not a plea.

A reminder.

She could handle him.

I let my hand lower.

Cassian: "Now?"

Haru: "Now."

Cassian shattered the right pylon.

The lake current reversed.

Leon cut the pull.

Aira sealed the backlash.

Elira turned Lucien's own ring into an opening and took the final crest fragment with two fingers.

The center gate blazed silver-blue.

**AURELIS: FINAL FRAGMENT SECURED**

The arena erupted.

But the event was not over.

The fragments still had to be returned to the center gate.

And Lucien was already reaching for the field beneath us.

Haru: "Move."

We moved.

Platforms collapsed behind us in sequence.

Not illegal.

Not obviously.

Just precise enough to be cruel.

I called the path as fast as I could without sounding impossible.

Haru: "Left. Stop. Jump. Leon, anchor. Aira, shield low. Cassian, do not hit that."

Cassian: "I was considering it."

Haru: "Yes."

Elira ran beside me, crest fragment in hand, golden light bright around her shoulders.

For one second, across the chaos, we moved in perfect rhythm.

Not because I controlled the path.

Because she trusted the part I could say aloud and understood the part I could not.

We reached the center gate with Noctis half a platform behind us.

Lucien lifted one hand.

The young Noctis student he had corrected the day before stumbled in front of Cassian's path, pushed by a lightless current disguised as lake backlash.

If Cassian collided with him, Aurelis would take an interference penalty.

If Cassian stopped, Noctis would pass.

Cassian saw it too late.

I saw it early.

So did Leon.

Leon threw a water ribbon around Cassian's waist and yanked him sideways hard enough to make him swear.

Cassian: "Varek!"

Leon: "You're welcome!"

Aira caught the Noctis student with a barrier instead of letting him fall.

Elira placed the crest fragments into the gate.

The gate accepted them.

Light shot upward.

**AURELIS ARCANE ACADEMY: CREST RELAY COMPLETE**

The timer froze.

Noctis arrived two seconds later.

Two seconds.

In a tournament like this, two seconds could become history.

The arena thundered.

I looked at Lucien.

He looked at the scoreboard, then at Leon, then at Aira, then finally at me.

His expression did not show anger.

That made me trust it less.

Lucien: "Interesting team."

Haru: "Functional."

Lucien's eyes cooled.

Lucien: "For now."

The judges' final scoring display formed overhead.

**OPENING EVENT RESULTS**

**1. Aurelis Arcane Academy**

**2. Noctis Dominion College**

**3. Valemere Royal Institute**

**4. Sol Aeternum Conservatory**

**5. Drakenshard Military Academy**

The Aurelis section exploded into celebration.

Aira looked like she might collapse from relief and insult the floor for catching her.
