The corridor stopped with her.

President Valmont: "Aurelis is unusually patient for an academy whose protected student is being approached by the same delegation accused of coercive conduit use."

Cael looked at her then.

Not with relief.

Not exactly.

Relief was too simple for someone who had learned not to trust doors just because they opened.

He looked at her like a person trying to remember whether protection could be real before punishment arrived to correct it.

Rhea saw that too.

Her voice cooled.

President Valmont: "Hearing hall. Now."

Magister Rusk: "We did not request a student council hearing."

President Valmont: "I know."

She turned and started walking again.

President Valmont: "That is why I arranged one."

---

Rhea did not move immediately after the Noctis officials turned toward the tower stairs.

For one heartbeat, her hand rested against the inside of her council sleeve, where a folded record seal had been stitched flat beneath the lining. I would not have noticed if the red mana around her wrist had not dimmed around it.

Seraphine noticed.

Lady Seraphine: "Still carrying that?"

Rhea's eyes stayed on Cael.

President Valmont: "It is useful."

Seraphine's expression did not soften, but her voice lowered.

Lady Seraphine: "It is old."

President Valmont: "So is the mistake."

The seal was not opened, but the memory around it felt sharp enough to cut through the corridor noise: a student hearing seven years ago, a Blue Circle girl accused of insubordination after refusing a family-sponsored combat trial, three faculty signatures declaring the matter private, and a younger Rhea Valmont standing outside the chamber with no authority to enter. The girl had left Aurelis before winter. The record had called it voluntary withdrawal.

Rhea had never believed that.

Her fingers closed once, precise and controlled.

President Valmont: "I learned the difference between procedure and protection too late for one student."

Seraphine said nothing.

Rhea straightened her sleeve until the hidden seal disappeared again.

President Valmont: "I will not learn it twice."

Then she walked toward the adjudication hall, and this time her authority looked less like rank than memory given teeth.

---

Rhea Valmont chose the Student Council adjudication hall because it was the worst possible room for Noctis to control quietly.

It sat above the east courtyard in a circular tower lined with glass, old red wood, and public witness sigils. Every student council decision that affected school safety could be recorded there under academy charter, with faculty oversight and student representation layered into the walls.

It was not a courtroom.

It had too many windows for secrets to feel comfortable.

Three semicircular tables faced the center floor. Rhea took the middle table with two council members I had not met. Lady Seraphine stood at the faculty table, arms folded, her expression severe enough to discourage decorative language. Professor Halden placed his containment tags into a sealed evidence tray and sat only after Seraphine pointed at the chair.

He obeyed.

Slowly.

But he obeyed.

Dren stood near the door, not seated, which told everyone in the room that he considered this a safety matter before an academic one.

The Noctis officials took the guest table.

Nera Vail stood behind them with Oric Sen at her shoulder.

Her binding rings were still.

Too still.

When Aira saw her, her pencil paused for the first time since we entered the hall.

Aira: "Their binder is here."

Leon: "Problem?"

Aira: "Witness."

Across the room, Nera's blue-gray eyes met Aira's for less than a second.

No greeting.

No alliance.

Only recognition between two support specialists who understood exactly how much damage could be hidden inside the word procedure.

Professor Valen took a faculty witness seat beside Halden with her folio case on her lap.

Halden glanced at her.

Professor Halden: "You knew this would happen."

Professor Valen: "I knew something would. I am glad it is happening where students can be protected instead of studied in pieces."

Halden looked away first.

Cael sat between Leon and Cassian.

That had not been announced.

It simply happened.

Leon placed himself to Cael's left, water mana quiet under his sleeve. Cassian took the right, one ankle crossed over the other as if he had wandered into the room for entertainment rather than positioning himself close enough to intercept any hostile gesture.

Aira sat behind them with three pages already divided into columns.

I recognized the headings from the way her pencil moved.

What we can say.

What we do not say.

What makes Noctis look worse if they ask.

Elira sat beside me.

The pendant beneath her uniform was quiet.

Too quiet.

That meant it was listening.

Rhea lifted one hand.

The witness sigils in the glass walls came alive, red at the edges and clear at the center.

The public gallery above the hall filled faster than protocol expected.

Green Circle students entered in quiet pairs and small groups until the upper benches held more green crests than any official academy proceeding I had ever seen. At the front sat Mira Castellan, curly auburn hair escaping its tie, worn notebook open across her knees.

One student beside her whispered too loudly.

Green Student: "Why are we here? This is not about us."

Mira did not look down from the hearing floor.

Mira: "It is exactly about us. If they judge him for being different, they are judging all of us for not being enough."

Another student swallowed.

Green Student: "What if he really is dangerous?"

Mira's pencil steadied.

Mira: "Then I want to see it myself. Not hear it from people who have never been Green."

When her eyes found mine across the room, she nodded once.

Not worship.

Not demand.

Witness.

President Valmont: "Emergency student safety proceeding. Subject: Noctis Dominion College demands regarding Cael Nox, tournament record integrity, and classification inquiry request naming Haru Aerlen."

The words appeared in the air above the center floor.

No rank beside my name.

No circle color.

Rhea had removed it.

That small mercy was tactical enough to survive scrutiny.

Magister Rusk: "For the record, Noctis Dominion objects to this venue."

President Valmont: "Objection recorded."

Magister Rusk: "This proceeding lacks interschool jurisdiction."

President Valmont: "Also recorded."

Magister Rusk: "Then why are we here?"

Rhea looked at him without blinking.

President Valmont: "Because you followed an injured student into a restricted corridor with an active record slate and an undeclared case after issuing a demand for his return. Aurelis has jurisdiction over its halls, its students, its guests, and its emergency safety measures."

The witness sigils wrote every word.

I watched the official realize that arguing would create more record.

He chose a different blade.

Magister Rusk: "Student Nox may clarify the matter quickly. He is a member of our delegation and can state that he wishes to return."

Cael's fingers closed around the edge of his chair.

The healer seal flickered.

Leon did not touch him.

Cassian did not speak.

Elira leaned forward slightly.

Haru: "He was already asked."

The official turned to me.

Magister Rusk: "By you."

Haru: "Yes."

Magister Rusk: "A Green Circle transfer student with unexplained involvement in a record gap."

There it was.

Not accusation.

Not yet.

A hook dressed as description.

My public Green Circle warmed faintly at my chest, ordinary and harmless-looking.

I let it.

Haru: "A witness."

Magister Rusk: "A convenient witness."

Professor Halden spoke before I could.

Professor Halden: "Convenience is not a disqualifying condition. If it were, most institutional claims would collapse immediately."

Aira's pencil paused.

Aira whispered, barely audible.

Aira: "I hate when he is useful."

Seraphine's mouth twitched once.

Rhea lifted two fingers.

President Valmont: "Student Nox will answer only questions approved by this chair or faculty oversight. He is injured. He is under healer protection. He is not evidence property."

The last sentence hit Cael harder than the rest.

He looked down at his own wrist.

For one heartbeat, the room's polished floor reflected him as two people: one sitting under Noctis black, and one under Aurelis red-white protection.

Then he raised his head.

Cael: "I do not wish to return to Noctis delegation supervision."

The words were not loud.

They did not need to be.

The witness sigils took them and hung them in the air in clean script.

**I DO NOT WISH TO RETURN TO NOCTIS DELEGATION SUPERVISION.**

Cael stared at the sentence as if it belonged to someone braver.

Magister Rusk: "Student Nox, you are under stress and external influence."

Cael: "Yes."

The official's eyes sharpened.

Cael swallowed once.

Cael: "I am under stress because Lucien Voss used me as a conduit without consent during Crown Field Team Combat. I am under external influence because Noctis trained me to call that loyalty."

The room did not fall silent.

It did something better.

It moved.

Aira wrote furiously. Leon's water mana steadied into a thin line beneath the table. Cassian's jaw tightened, humor gone. Seraphine took one step closer to the evidence tray. Halden's containment tags brightened as if recognizing the shape of the testimony.

Rhea let all of it happen before she spoke.

President Valmont: "Statement entered."

Magister Rusk: "Noctis objects to emotionally contaminated testimony."

The phrase touched the witness sigils before Rhea answered.

For half a second, Cael's sentence and Rusk's objection hung side by side in the air.

One looked like a person speaking.

The other looked like a label trying to cover him.

Rhea's red crest flared once, disciplined and exact.

President Valmont: "Noctis objection entered as: delegation disputes student account after demand for student's return."

Magister Rusk: "That is not what I said."

Aira lifted a hand.

President Valmont: "Miss Solen?"

Aira looked surprised for half a second, then recovered like someone who had been waiting her entire life for bureaucracy to become a weapon.

Aira: "Clarifying question for the Noctis delegation."

Rhea nodded.

Aira turned one page around so the room could see her columns.

Aira: "What is the official Noctis procedure for a conduit-support student to refuse participation in a live tournament technique?"

The official's expression did not change.

His aide's did.

Small mistake.

But Aira saw it.

So did Rhea.

So did Halden.

Magister Rusk: "Noctis training contracts define role obligations before field entry."

Aira: "It was not the question."

Cassian murmured beside Cael.

Cassian: "She has teeth today."

Leon: "She always has teeth."

Cassian: "Today she brought paperwork."

Aira did not look back, but her shoulders lifted by one proud millimeter.

Aira: "If Cael says no during a match, what procedure protects him?"

Magister Rusk: "A combat conduit cannot disrupt formation because of momentary discomfort."

Cael's face tightened.

Not pale.

Not frozen.

Angry.

It was the healthiest expression I had seen on him all day.

Aira circled something on her page.

Aira: "So no procedure."

Magister Rusk: "That is an unserious interpretation."

Professor Halden: "It is concise."

Seraphine: "And likely accurate."

Magister Rusk: "Aurelis faculty appears eager to coach student rhetoric."

Lady Seraphine: "No. We teach it before emergencies."

Rhea's eyes moved to Cael.

President Valmont: "Student Nox, do you want to amend your statement?"

Cael looked at the Noctis table.

His fingers trembled.

He did not hide them.

Cael: "Yes."

The witness sigils waited.

Cael: "I do not wish to return to Noctis delegation supervision because I do not believe refusal is recognized there as something I am allowed to have."

The script appeared overhead.

This time, he did not look away from it.

---

The classification inquiry came next because Noctis understood how to retreat without stepping backward.

The official let Cael's testimony hang where everyone could see it, then opened the black case.

The latch clicked once.

Every witness sigil in the glass walls dimmed, as if the room had blinked.

Inside was a narrow prism of dark glass suspended in silver brackets.

It did not rise.

It did not flare.

That made it worse.

The shard simply waited in its velvet hollow while the hall bent around it.

Reflections in the glass walls warped first. Rhea's red crest stretched into a thin crown. Halden's face split into two pale profiles, one watching the shard and one watching me. In the upper gallery, the Green Circle students became a row of green rings without faces for half a breath before the glass corrected itself.

The cold pressure behind my sternum sharpened.

Elira felt the change through the pendant.

Her fingers found mine beneath the table.

No one else saw.

Cael did.

Not our hands.

The case.

His chair leg scraped the floor before he could stop himself.

The healer seal on his wrist pulled toward the open case, blue-white threads stretching from the band like wet silk caught on a hook.

Cael clamped his other hand over it.

Not because it hurt.

Because he recognized the feeling.

Leon shifted half an inch closer. Cassian's crossed ankle dropped flat.

Magister Rusk: "Noctis Dominion submits a Foundation Relay preservation shard."

Halden stood.

Professor Halden: "Undeclared old-system artifact in a student safety hearing."

Magister Rusk: "Preservation shard. Non-hostile. Record function only."

Professor Halden: "That is what most hostile artifacts say when translated by their owners."

Rhea looked at Halden.

President Valmont: "Assessment."

Halden did not touch the shard.

For once, he did not even seem tempted.

He opened his black case and removed one of the containment tags from the mirror chamber. The tag's edge still carried a burned line where Lucien's lightless revision had tried to write through it.

Professor Halden: "It resonates with the same doctrine branch the Aurelis Foundation Record named below the east archive."

The Noctis official's eyes flicked toward him.

Too fast.

Halden caught it.

Professor Halden: "Dominion variant."

The witness sigils recorded the phrase.

Halden's expression darkened as the containment tag blackened at one corner.

Professor Halden: "Old system. Dangerous."

He pointed at the pulsing relic without touching the glass.

Professor Halden: "It does not simply preserve records. It preserves the version of a record its doctrine can force the room to accept."

The shard inside the case pulsed.

A pale ring opened within it.

Not Yellow.

Not any public Circle rank.

A thin institutional circle, gray-white and cold, carrying authority without warmth.

Frost feathered across the inside of the black case.

The velvet beneath the shard turned white, then black again, as if the cold had learned to hide.

The red witness sigils along the glass walls bent toward it, not touching, like flames leaning away from bad air.

In the gallery, Mira pressed one hand over the page on her notebook to keep it from curling.

Her pencil rolled off the bench.

No one bent to pick it up.

Then the shard rang.

Not loud.

A single clear note, too low to be heard with the ears alone.

The circular hall answered physically.

Glass panels bowed inward. The red witness sigils stretched thin and colorless. Every reflection in the room lost its face and kept only a crest: Green, Blue, Red, Yellow, all flattened into evidence.

Cael made a sound like he had swallowed a shout. His healer seal dragged his wrist two inches across the table before Leon caught the chair, not the boy, and braced it with his knee.

Elira's pendant went hot against her chest.

My visible Green Circle flickered once.

For one fraction of a breath, the hidden Black Circle beneath it pressed back.

The floor stopped trembling.

The faces returned to the glass.

But everyone had seen the room try to turn us into colors.

The air above the center floor filled with text.

**NOCTIS FOUNDATION RELAY**

**DELEGATION INTEGRITY REVIEW**

**CONDUIT RETURN STATUS: CONTESTED**

**CLASSIFICATION INQUIRY: HARU AERLEN**

My name appeared again.

No circle beside it.

That should have been comforting.

It was not.

The shard was waiting for the missing word.

The black case sat open in the middle of a student safety hearing.

Cael's healer seal strained toward it.

Every reflection in the room looked briefly less like a person than a possible classification.

Magister Rusk: "Haru Aerlen's actions during Crown Field Team Combat remain unexplained. Cael Nox suffered conduit collapse. Records show a seven-second gap. Aurelis claims Noctis interference. Noctis requests classification review to determine whether an unauthorized power category affected a delegation student."

The accusation was built beautifully.

It did not call me Black Circle.

It did not need to.

It only created a room where refusing the question would look like guilt.

Halden's earlier words returned to me.

Denial or possession.

Most institutions chose one.

Noctis had chosen both.

Elira's hand tightened around mine once, then released.

Letting me answer.

Trusting me to choose the shape of the truth.

Rhea's gaze moved to me.

President Valmont: "Haru Aerlen may answer within student protection limits."

I stood.

The visible Green Circle at my chest gave off its harmless light.

The Noctis shard noticed.

The hidden Black Circle beneath it did not move.

It was the most dangerous choice I made all day.

Haru: "During Crown Field Team Combat, I read a pressure failure forming around Cael Nox."

The shard pulsed.

**FIELD READING CLAIM REGISTERED**

Magister Rusk: "A Green Circle field reading does not account for survival after a lightless conduit collapse."

Haru: "I did not say it accounted for everything."

The official leaned forward.

Magister Rusk: "Then account for everything."

There were many ways to lose a secret.

Panic was only one.

Pride was another.

So was kindness, if kindness forgot consequence.

I looked at Cael.

He was watching me with an expression I understood too well.

He did not know my full secret.

But he knew what it meant to become the place where other people aimed their systems.

I would not save him by stepping into the same cage and calling it mercy.

Haru: "No."

Cael flinched.

Only a little.

Enough.

I had aimed the refusal at Noctis, but he had heard the shape of an old answer.

My mistake landed before the sigils could record anything useful.

I forced my voice lower.

Haru: "No to the frame. Not no to you."

Cael's fingers loosened around the edge of Leon's mantle.

The witness sigils brightened.

The Noctis official's face hardened.

Magister Rusk: "You refuse classification inquiry."

Haru: "I refuse an inquiry framed to make my classification answer for Noctis misconduct."

The shard flickered.

Halden's eyes sharpened.

Rhea's mouth did not move, but her council seal warmed at her palm.

Haru: "Question one: Did Lucien Voss use Cael Nox as a conduit without clear refusal protection?"

The script overhead adjusted.

Magister Rusk: "You are not chairing this hearing."

Haru: "Question two: Did Noctis records attempt to rewrite the seven missing seconds after Cael gave testimony?"

The mirror containment tag in Halden's hand flashed.

Haru: "Question three: Why does the Noctis Foundation Relay care more about naming me than protecting him?"

The shard pulsed so hard that every glass panel in the adjudication hall trembled.

For one breath, old pressure crawled under the floor.

Not Aurelis.

Noctis.

Cold doctrine moving through a legal opening.

**DOMINION REVIEW PRESSURE DETECTED**

**SUBJECT HARU AERLEN: RESPONSE RESISTANT**

I felt the hidden Black Circle turn its attention toward the words.

Not open.

Not reveal.

Attend.

It was enough to make the room colder.

Elira's pendant warmed beneath her uniform, but it did not activate.

Because the threat had not touched her.

Because the pendant understood boundaries better than most institutions.

The official looked at the shard as if it had betrayed him by speaking too clearly.

Professor Halden stepped into the center floor.

Professor Halden: "For the record, the Noctis relay is attempting classification pressure before establishing injury causation."

Magister Rusk: "Professor Halden-"

Professor Halden: "It is also responding to Mr. Aerlen's refusal to let classification replace sequence."

Rhea lifted her hand.

President Valmont: "Entered."

The witness sigils wrote it.

The Noctis official closed the case.

Too late.

The shard's text remained in the air for three seconds after the lid shut.

Long enough for every person in the hall to read it.

Long enough for Aira to copy it exactly.

Long enough for Cael to see that Noctis had not asked whether he was safe.

Only whether I could be named.

---

Lucien arrived without opening a door.

He came through the oath crystal above the guest table, a distortion of pale light where the witness sigils crossed the Noctis delegation's submitted records.

Not a full projection.

Not enough to count as attendance.

Enough to be disrespectful.

His outline formed like a reflection choosing to disobey the mirror.

Ash-blond hair.

Pale eyes.

The suggestion of a smile that had never needed warmth to be effective.

The Noctis official turned sharply.

Magister Rusk: "Voss."

Lucien: "You were taking too long."

The room changed shape around that voice.

Not through silence.

Through decisions.

Seraphine moved between Lucien's reflection and the student seats. Dren's hand dropped to the combat seal at his belt. Leon's water line spread under Cael's chair. Cassian's fire appeared as a thin red edge along the table, low and controlled. Rhea's council seal climbed her forearm in precise rings.

Elira stood.

So did I.

Lucien's gaze found her first.

Lucien: "Elira Vale. Still standing beside damaged things and calling it principle."

The pendant at her collarbone became impossible to touch.

Not bright.

Not visible to everyone.

Only present.

Elira: "Still mistaking cruelty for clarity."

Lucien's smile deepened.

Lucien: "Clarity is what remains when mercy stops interrupting."

Haru: "Then you have never seen clearly."

His eyes moved to me.

There it was.

The pressure behind the words.

Not anger.

Interest sharpened into a tool.

Lucien: "Haru Aerlen. The Green Circle who refuses to explain why old systems hesitate around him."

Haru: "The Yellow Circle who needs injured students to hold his shape."

Cael's breath caught.

Lucien did not look at him.

It was the cruelty of it.

Not rage.

Disinterest.

Lucien: "Cael was assigned a role."

Cael's hands closed.

This time, his voice arrived before anyone protected him from needing it.

Cael: "I am not your role."

Lucien's eyes shifted.

Finally.

The lightless reflection flickered at the edges.

Lucien: "Aurelis has made you dramatic."

Cael: "No. Aurelis asked me a question."

Lucien: "And you mistook the novelty for freedom."

Rhea's seal flared.

President Valmont: "Lucien Voss, you are not recognized as a participant in this proceeding."

Lucien: "I know."

He looked at the witness sigils.

Lucien: "That is why the proceeding is useful."

The Noctis shard inside the closed case began to ring.

Low.

Cold.

Like glass remembering a bell.

Halden's containment tags lifted from the evidence tray.

Professor Halden: "The relay is using his unauthorized projection as a bridge."

Aira's pencil snapped.

Aira: "It is."

She grabbed another from behind her ear.

Aira: "I hate prepared villains."

Lucien's reflection spread one hand.

The oath crystal above the table fractured into pale lines.

Text appeared across the room, not in Aurelis red but in Noctis gray.

**DOMINION DOCTRINE: WITNESS CLAIM CONTESTED**

**CONDUIT TESTIMONY STATUS: REBELLIOUS**

**AURELIS PROTECTION CLAIM: CHALLENGED**

Rhea's council sigils slammed against the text.

The air sparked red and silver.

Dren moved.

Seraphine moved faster.

She struck the faculty table with two fingers, activating an emergency seal that folded around the student seats like transparent armor.

Not to trap us.

To keep Noctis out.

Lucien watched it with faint amusement.

Lucien: "Aurelis keeps building shelters and mistaking them for answers."

Elira: "Shelter is an answer when someone is being hunted."

His gaze touched the pendant.

For a fraction of a second, lightless mana reached toward it.

The pendant did not flare.

It refused.

Not with power.

With ownership.

Lucien's fingers stopped as if the air between him and Elira had become law.

His smile thinned.

I felt the Black Circle stir.

Not because he had threatened me.

Because he had reached toward her again.

My public Green Circle stayed soft.

Ordinary.

Small.

I held the rest behind my ribs until restraint felt like gripping the edge of a blade.

Haru: "Do not touch her."

The words were quiet.

Lucien heard the part beneath them.

So did Elira.

Her hand found my sleeve.

Not to stop me.

To remind me I had already chosen who I was.

Lucien's eyes brightened.

Lucien: "There it is."

Noctis text sharpened.

**RESPONSE PRESSURE DETECTED**

**CLASSIFICATION-**

Halden threw the mirror containment tag into the text.

It struck like a blade through ink.

The word broke before it finished.

Professor Halden: "No."

Everyone looked at him.

Halden's face had gone colorless with fear.

But he did not step back.

Professor Halden: "You will not use my review to name a student through hostile pressure."

Lucien studied him with new interest.

Lucien: "Professor Halden. How disappointing. I thought curiosity was your better instinct."

Professor Halden: "It is."

He adjusted his glasses with a hand that trembled once and then steadied.

Professor Halden: "That is why I am learning when not to feed it."

For one second, I understood why the old Aurelis system had accepted him as witness.

Not because he was kind.

Because he could want the answer and still refuse to steal it.

The broken Noctis text recoiled.

Rhea seized the opening.

President Valmont: "Student Council emergency ruling: Cael Nox remains under Aurelis healer and council protection pending interschool review. No private Noctis access. No conduit reassignment. No classification inquiry into any Aurelis student will proceed under Noctis relay pressure."

Her seal struck the center floor.

**RULING ENTERED**

The glass walls blazed red.

Students watching from the lower courtyard would see only emergency light.

Not my secret.

Not the old text.

Not how close the word classification had come to becoming something worse.

Lucien looked at the ruling.

Then at me.

Lucien: "You keep surviving by letting others speak for you."

Haru: "No."

I looked at Cael.

At Aira's broken pencil.

At Leon and Cassian flanking a student their own school would have called enemy two days ago.

At Halden standing between curiosity and possession.

At Rhea turning authority into shelter.

At Elira, who knew the full truth and still let me choose how much of it the room could bear.

Haru: "I survive because they are allowed to speak."

Lucien's expression changed.

Only slightly.

Enough.

---

The Aurelis Foundation Record answered from beneath the floor.

It should not have been able to.

The Record Hall was below the east archive, sealed behind old passages and council exemptions.

But the adjudication hall had witness sigils older than most current academy charters, and old systems recognized old responsibilities when pressure forced them close enough to touch.

The red council light dimmed.

Gold, blue, red, silver, and green points appeared across the floor around us.

Not circles.

Positions.

Witness marks.

Aira: "Please tell me the building is on our side."

Professor Halden looked down, eyes wide with controlled panic.

Professor Halden: "Buildings do not take sides."

Cassian: "This one absolutely has opinions."

The floor answered.

**AURELIS FOUNDATION RECORD: WITNESS RESPONSE ACCEPTED**

**SAFEGUARD CANDIDATE: NOT ISOLATED**

**CONDUIT: SELF-DECLARED**

Cael looked at the word self.

His mouth parted.

No sound came out at first.

Then:

Cael: "It heard me."

Elira's voice softened.

Elira: "Yes."

The Record shifted.

**DOMINION CLAIM REJECTED IN AURELIS JURISDICTION**

Rhea allowed herself one slow breath.

Then Noctis answered.

The closed shard in the black case cracked.

A line of pale-gray light cut up through the lid, through the air, and into the witness sigils.

Not strong enough to overtake them.

Strong enough to write.

**NOCTIS FOUNDATION RELAY: DOMINION CLAIM PRESERVED**

**CONTEST MAY BE TRANSFERRED TO TOURNAMENT AUTHORITY**

The Noctis official went very still.

Not because he had expected it.

Because he had not.

Lucien's reflection smiled.

It was when I understood.

The officials had come to pressure us.

Lucien had come to make the pressure public enough that a higher system would have to respond.

He did not need to win the hearing.

He needed to move the battlefield.

Text unfolded in the air between the two foundation echoes.

**INTER-NATIONAL SCHOOLS TOURNAMENT: FOUNDATION DISPUTE PROTOCOL RECOGNIZED**

**AURELIS PROTECTION CLAIM VS NOCTIS DOMINION CLAIM**

**WITNESS TRIAL AUTHORIZED**

Rhea's expression hardened.

Lady Seraphine swore very quietly.

Dren looked ready to punch a legal concept.

Aira wrote the words down, then underlined trial so hard the page tore.

Leon: "What is a witness trial?"

No one answered immediately.

It was answer enough.

Halden read the next line aloud, voice thin with understanding.

Professor Halden: "A sanctioned proceeding where competing institutional records test which account preserves the most complete truth under pressure."

Cassian: "So a trial pretending to be a duel."

Seraphine: "It is worse. A duel only lets people hit each other."

The old text continued.

**CLAIMANT WITNESSES REQUIRED**

**CONDUIT TESTIMONY CENTRAL**

**CLASSIFICATION PRESSURE PROHIBITED UNLESS VOLUNTARILY ENTERED**

That last line gave me room to breathe.

Then the next appeared.

**PRIMARY AURELIS FIELD WITNESS REQUESTED: HARU AERLEN**

Elira's hand found mine again.

This time everyone saw.

No one commented.

Lucien's reflection watched our joined hands with interest that felt like a blade deciding where to rest.

Lucien: "There. A fair procedure."

Cael stood so abruptly Leon had to move with him to keep the healer seal from tugging.

Cael: "There is nothing fair about you choosing the room."

Lucien: "I did not choose it. I merely understood what the rooms wanted."

Haru: "No."

Lucien's eyes moved back to me.

Haru: "You understood what pressure could force them to open. That is not the same thing."

For the first time, his smile did not return immediately.

Good.

Rhea read the authorization again.

President Valmont: "Classification pressure prohibited unless voluntarily entered."

Her gaze came to me.

The question was clear.

Could I enter without exposing everything?

The honest answer was dangerous.

The useful answer was not yet.

Haru: "I can be field witness."

Elira's fingers tightened.

Haru: "Not classification subject."

Halden nodded once.

Professor Halden: "That distinction must be written into the acceptance."

Rhea was already writing.

President Valmont: "It will be."

Magister Rusk: "Noctis does not accept unilateral modification."

Rhea did not look at him.

President Valmont: "Then Noctis can explain to tournament authority why it objects to prohibiting classification pressure in a witness trial about student safety."

Aira pointed her broken pencil at the official.

Aira: "Please do. I want to hear it out loud."

Rusk said nothing.

For once, his gloves looked less clean.

Frost from the case had left a pale dusting along the knuckles.

Lucien laughed softly.

Lucien: "Careful, Aurelis. If you build your shield out of mercy, someone will eventually ask whether it can survive impact."

Elira looked directly at him.

Elira: "Then we will show them."

Gold light touched the edge of her visible Yellow Circle.

Not attack.

Resolve.

Lucien's projection began to fray as Rhea's council seal forced the unauthorized bridge closed.

The glass panels behind him tried to keep his reflection and failed. For a moment there were six Luciens in the wall, each thinner than the last, each smiling a fraction too late.

Before it vanished, his gaze returned to me one final time.

Lucien: "Three days, Haru Aerlen."

The air around my name tightened.

Lucien: "Bring your witnesses. Bring your restraint. Bring whatever name you are hiding from the record."

Lucien: "Dominion is very good at making people prove what they are."

Rhea's council rings snapped shut.

The six reflections collapsed into one pale line.

Then he was gone.

The oath crystal cracked in half.

A sliver fell onto the guest table and spun until Seraphine stopped it with the tip of one finger.

No one moved for several seconds.

But this time, the pause did not feel like shock.

It felt like everyone counting what had to be done before the next strike landed.

The gallery began to empty in uneven waves, but Mira Castellan came down instead.

No one stopped her. Maybe because she was small beside the council tables and moved like someone expecting to be told she had misunderstood permission. Maybe because the room had just watched institutions fight over who counted as a person, and even guards knew better than to interrupt a Green Circle student walking toward another one.

She stopped at the edge of the center floor, notebook hugged to her chest. The paper smelled faintly of ink and crushed mint leaves, the cheap kind students used to keep themselves awake during late study.

Mira: "Haru Aerlen?"

I turned.

Haru: "Yes."

Her freckles stood out against a face that had gone pale from nerves, but her eyes stayed on mine.

Mira: "Are you afraid?"

The question slipped past every prepared answer Aira had built for me.

Elira's hand brushed mine beneath the table. She did not answer for me.

Haru: "Yes."

Mira nodded as if that mattered more than courage.

Mira: "Good. I mean... not good. But real." She tightened her grip on the notebook. "Some Green Circle students are pretending they came because they love politics. They did not. We came because when people talk about weak circles, they never mean our magic first. They mean our right to be believed."

My throat tightened.

Mira looked down at her worn shoes, then back up.

Mira: "If they can make your explanation disappear because your crest is Green, they can do it to any of us. So we are writing things down. Names. Questions. What people actually said before officials rearrange it."

She held out a page torn cleanly from the notebook. Several names filled the bottom, each written in different handwriting.

Mira: "We are not asking you to be a symbol. That seems exhausting. We just want you to know the gallery is not empty."

I accepted the page carefully.

Haru: "Thank you, Mira."

She blinked at her name, surprised I knew it.

Haru: "And I am afraid."

This time, I let myself smile.

Haru: "But less than before."

Aira turned to a fresh page.

Aira: "All right."

Her voice shook.

Only once.

Then it steadied.

Aira: "New protocol. We need witness statements, field maps, permitted answers, forbidden answers, a healer plan for Cael, legal language for Haru, and snacks."

Cassian looked at her.

Cassian: "Snacks?"

Aira: "If we are going to fight a legal duel against a haunted Noctis institution, I refuse to do it hungry."

Leon sat back down beside Cael.

Leon: "Agreed."

Cassian: "I love this team."

Cael looked at them as if the word team still did not know whether it was allowed to include him.

Then Cassian nudged the empty space on the table toward him.

Cassian: "You too, conduit witness."

Cael looked down.

The healer seal at his wrist steadied.

Cael: "I do not know how to do this."

Elira's voice was gentle.

Elira: "Neither did we."

I looked at the fading text above the floor.

**WITNESS TRIAL AUTHORIZED**

Three days.

A public proceeding.

A foundation dispute.

Noctis trying to turn mercy into evidence against us.

Lucien waiting inside the pressure.

And somewhere beneath my visible Green Circle, the hidden Black Circle remained quiet.

Not asleep.

Listening.

I had spent years believing secrecy meant standing alone.

Now an ancient record, a hostile relay, a student council president, suspicious faculty, frightened allies, and one enemy with pale eyes had all forced the opposite lesson into the open.

If I stood alone, I would become exactly what old systems wanted to name.

If I stood with witnesses, I might remain a person long enough to choose.

Elira's hand was still in mine.

This time, I did not let go.

Haru: "Then we prepare."

Rhea's red seal closed over the official record.

President Valmont: "Hearing adjourned."

Outside the glass walls, the academy bells began to ring for a class none of us had attended.

The sound moved over Aurelis like ordinary life trying to continue.

Briefly, it almost worked.

Then every tournament notice crystal across the courtyard turned black.

One line appeared in silver-white text.

**FOUNDATION WITNESS TRIAL: AURELIS ARCANE ACADEMY VS NOCTIS DOMINION COLLEGE**

Below it, two names formed.

**FIELD WITNESS: HARU AERLEN**

**DOMINION WITNESS: LUCIEN VOSS**

The courtyard erupted.

Not all at once.

First came the scrape of benches under the open arcade as students stood too quickly. Then a dropped lunch tray struck stone and sent silverware skidding across the courtyard. A Blue Circle student cursed under his breath. Two Green Circle first-years near the fountain grabbed each other's sleeves and stared up at Haru Aerlen written where tournament scores usually lived.

The repaired fountain rippled backward once.

No grand display.

Just one small reversal in the water, enough for the students closest to it to step away.

Mira saw it from the gallery stairs.

So did I.

In the adjudication hall, the public witness list expanded.

**NEUTRAL WITNESS: SOREN ASHVALE - VALEMERE ROYAL INSTITUTE**

Soren stepped forward from the gallery entrance in Valemere silver-blue, posture straight and hands visible.

Soren: "Valemere Royal Institute offers neutral witness testimony."

Lucien's name on the projection seemed to darken.

Lucien's distant voice slipped through the trial notice, amused and cold.

Lucien: "How convenient."

Soren did not look at the projection.

He looked at the witness sigils.

Soren: "No. How honorable."

Another line appeared beneath Noctis' witness list.

**NOCTIS TECHNICAL WITNESS: NERA VAIL**

Nera stood.

Her face was composed.

Her hands were not.

Aira stared at her, then leaned toward me.

Aira: "She is going to tell the truth."

Haru: "How do you know?"

Aira: "Because she looks terrified. Liars look confident."

In the upper gallery, Mira's notebook moved quickly. Around her, Green Circle students stopped whispering and started watching.

Beside Halden, Valen folded her hands over her folio.

Professor Halden: "You still believe this can help him."

Professor Valen: "No. I believe people can."

I looked at Elira.

She looked back at me, golden eyes steady, fear and faith held in the same breath.

The next battle would not ask how much power I had.

That would have been easier.

It would ask whether restraint could survive being judged in public.

And for the first time since arriving at Aurelis, I was not the only one who would have to answer.

---

Nera did not return to the Noctis common room after the hearing.

She stood in the narrow preparation chamber assigned to technical witnesses and stared at the binding rings on her wrists until the metal blurred.

Oric entered without announcing himself.

Oric: "You are not going to testify the way he wants."

Nera did not turn.

Nera: "I will testify truthfully."

Oric: "That is not the same thing."

Nera closed one hand. The ring clicked.

Nera: "No."

Oric leaned against the door, pale and tired and younger than he looked when Noctis formation lines told him where to stand.

Oric: "Lucien will know."

Nera: "I know."

The silence between them carried every order they had obeyed.

Oric: "I will stand with you. If you are going to tell the truth."

She looked at him then, surprise breaking discipline before she could hide it.

Oric: "I am tired too, Nera. I am just not as brave as you."

Nera's laugh was almost nothing.

Nera: "I am not brave."

She looked down at the rings again.

Nera: "I am just done watching."

---

Soren found me on the east balcony late that night.

I had gone there because the academy roofline was quiet, because the city lights below Aurelis did not ask questions, and because my room had a mirror I did not want to face.

Soren stopped at the archway.

Soren: "Mind company?"

Haru: "How did you find me?"

He smiled faintly.

Soren: "Duelists learn to read people. You have the look of someone preparing for a fight he does not want."

I looked back over the city.

Haru: "That obvious?"

Soren came to stand at the railing, leaving enough space between us to make the silence his own.

Soren: "I came to tell you Valemere will testify at the trial. We saw what Lucien did. We will say so."

Haru: "Why?"

Soren's answer took longer than I expected.

Soren: "Because Elira chose you."

I looked at him.

No challenge waited in his face.

Only honesty.

Soren: "I am not going to pretend I did not notice her. I did. But I also noticed how she looks at you. Like you are the only person in the room who sees her as more than a Yellow Circle."

The words landed too cleanly.

Soren: "Do not let Lucien make you doubt that. He will try. He will make it about power, rank, and whatever you are hiding. But it is really about whether you believe you deserve to be chosen."

Haru: "And if I do not?"

Soren's mouth curved.

Soren: "Then you are an idiot. But not, I think, a permanent one."

Despite everything, I almost laughed.

Soren: "When the trial opens, remember this. She already chose. You just have to let her keep choosing."

He left before I could decide how to answer.

For an honorable rival, he was extremely inconvenient.

---

Professor Valen found Elira before I did.

I saw them from the balcony stairs: Valen in comfortable robes, Elira still in uniform, the black pendant hidden beneath her collar and not hidden from either of them.

Professor Valen: "Whatever happens at the trial, you are not responsible for protecting him from every consequence."

Elira's voice was quiet.

Elira: "Then what am I responsible for?"

Professor Valen: "Standing beside him while he faces them."

Elira looked toward the dark glass of the windows.

Elira: "What if standing beside him is not enough?"

Valen touched her shoulder once.

Professor Valen: "Then it will still be more than he has ever had before."

Elira's hand closed over the pendant.

Elira: "He has a secret that could destroy him."

Professor Valen: "No. He has a truth that frightens people. There is a difference. Secrets are about shame. Truths are about fear."

Valen's eyes softened.

Professor Valen: "When the trial begins, make them face their fear instead of his shame."

When Valen left, Elira turned and saw me.

She did not pretend she had not been waiting.

Elira: "You are thinking about running."

Haru: "I am thinking about what happens if I do not."

She crossed the distance between us and took my face in both hands.

Her palms were cold.

I had been so busy measuring what my secret could do to everyone else that I had missed the simplest part.

She was afraid too.

Not of me.

For me.

And I had mistaken that fear for something I needed to solve from a distance.

Elira: "Then think about this."

She kissed me.

Not gentle.

Not tentative.

Desperate and certain at once, as if fear had finally stopped pretending it was the opposite of love.

When she drew back, her eyes were bright.

Elira: "Three days from now, you will stand in that trial circle. Lucien will try to make you choose between hiding and protecting us. But you have already chosen. You chose when you let me see you. You chose when you saved Cael. You chose every time you stood with us instead of above us."

Haru: "What if choosing costs you everything?"

Elira: "Then we will have nothing. Together."

The answer broke something in me that had needed breaking for years.

She drew the black pendant from beneath her collar.

Elira: "You gave me this because you thought you could not always be beside me."

She placed my hand over the pendant, over her heart.

Elira: "But you are wrong about one thing. You are never not beside me. Even when you are hiding. Even when you are afraid. Even when you think protecting me means distance."

Her fingers covered mine.

Elira: "So stop practicing how to lose me. Start practicing how to let me fight for you."

Outside, the academy bells rang midnight.

Three days until the trial.

Three days until the world demanded an answer.

But in that moment, there were no ranks. No circles. No ancient systems or hostile institutions.

Only her fingers still trembling against mine after she had been brave enough to hold me still.

Just two people who had chosen each other when the world told them not to.

And that choice, more than any hidden power, was what Lucien would never understand.


===== EPISODE 16: The Trial That Refused a Name =====

# Episode 16: **The Trial That Refused a Name**

Three days passed like Aurelis was holding its breath.

The academy still taught classes.

It still served breakfast.

It still rang bells, assigned readings, posted practice schedules, and expected students to pretend that normal life was not something everyone had agreed to perform badly.

But every reflective surface on campus had learned a new habit.

Windows held onto shadows for too long. Fountain water stilled whenever a Noctis uniform crossed the courtyard. Notice crystals went blank for one heartbeat before displaying harmless announcements. Even the polished floor outside the dining hall reflected colored circles under students' feet before remembering not to.

The old systems were awake now.

They were trying to listen politely.

That was almost worse than if they had screamed.

On the morning of the Foundation Witness Trial, I woke before the bell and sat on the edge of my bed with both hands resting on my knees.

The room smelled faintly of rain through the open window and old paper from the stack of witness protocols Aira had left on my desk.

My false Green Circle rested quietly near my heart.

Under it, the hidden Black Circle did not sleep.

It waited.

Not eagerly.

Not fearfully.

Like a blade left sheathed because everyone in the room knew it existed except the people shouting for proof.

I dressed slowly.

Green-trimmed uniform. Academy mantle. Plain boots. No ornament that could be mistaken for confidence.

The mirror above the washstand reflected a transfer student with dark hair, tired eyes, and a Green Circle crest harmless enough that most of the world would have stopped looking there.

Most of the world had not met Lucien Voss.

A knock came at my door.

Not loud.

Three precise taps.

Elira.

I opened it.

She stood in the hallway in full Aurelis formal uniform, the gold of her Yellow Circle crest bright beneath her collar. Her hair was tied back with a white ribbon instead of left loose, which should have made her look more distant.

It did not.

It made her look ready.

The pendant was hidden beneath her uniform.

I felt it anyway.

Elira: "Good morning."

Haru: "Is it?"

Her mouth softened.

Elira: "No. But it is ours."

That answer did more for my breathing than any calming spell would have.

She reached up and straightened the edge of my mantle.

Elira: "Aira is already in the council preparation room. Leon is pretending not to hover over Cael. Cassian has threatened two procedural documents. Rhea has not smiled once."

Haru: "So everyone is calm."

Elira: "Terribly."

For one breath, we stood there in the quiet hallway, close enough that I could hear the small shift in her breathing.

Elira: "You do not have to look unafraid."

Haru: "I know."

She looked at me.

I corrected myself.

Haru: "I understand."

That was not quite true either.

I understood the words. I understood the strategy. I understood that fear, displayed honestly, would not automatically become a weapon in the hands of everyone who saw it.

My body had not received the memo.

Elira tugged once at the edge of my sleeve.

Elira: "You are doing it again."

Haru: "What?"

Elira: "Turning feelings into an assignment."

I looked down at the mantle she had straightened.

Haru: "It is a difficult habit."

Elira: "I know. That is why I am naming it before the trial does."

Elira's fingers brushed mine.

Elira: "Better."

We walked toward the council wing together.

No one stopped us.

But students watched.

Green Circle students most of all.

Not with worship.

Not even with certainty.

With the careful attention of people who knew that if the world could turn one harmless-looking crest into a question, it could do the same to theirs.

That kind of attention was heavier than suspicion.

Suspicion asked what I was.

Hope asked what I would do.

---

The preparation room beneath the Student Council tower had become Aira Solen's temporary kingdom.

Maps covered one wall. Witness rules covered another. Three tables had been pushed together, then immediately colonized by paper, tea, healer notes, legal summaries, mana flow diagrams, and a plate of small honey cakes that Cassian had apparently acquired under circumstances no one wanted entered into evidence.

Aira stood at the center of it all with a pencil behind one ear and the exhausted authority of a battlefield commander.

Aira: "Good. Couple unit has arrived."

Elira: "Please never call us that again."

Aira: "Denied for emotional efficiency."

Leon looked up from the chair beside Cael.

Leon: "She's been like this since dawn."

Cael sat with his healer seal visible over one wrist. The blue-white glow was steadier than it had been three days ago, but not strong. The red council mark around it pulsed whenever the room's trial wards shifted.

He looked better.

He was not healed.

That difference mattered.

Cassian leaned against the far table, arms folded, red crest low and controlled.

Cassian: "She made me practice saying 'objection' without smiling."

Aira: "You failed."

Cassian: "I improved from catastrophic to inappropriate."

Rhea Valmont stood near the door with Seraphine and Professor Valen. Rhea's red council seal had been formalized into three narrow rings around her forearm. They made no sound, but every Noctis relay crystal in the room turned slightly away from them.

Professor Halden stood by the evidence tray with both hands behind his back.

He had not touched the containment tags.

I noticed.

He noticed me noticing.

His expression said he found this entire moral development inconvenient.

Professor Halden: "Mr. Aerlen."

Haru: "Professor."

Professor Halden: "Your public explanation remains sequence-based. Do not offer classification language. Do not answer speculative color questions. Do not allow Noctis to define your action before causation is established."

Aira pointed at him with her pencil.

Aira: "That is my line, but drier."

Professor Halden: "Accuracy often is."

Mira Castellan entered carrying a green folio against her chest.

She stopped just inside the door when everyone looked at her.

Then she lifted her chin and kept walking.

Mira: "Green Circle witness notes."

Rhea's attention sharpened.

President Valmont: "From the gallery?"

Mira nodded.

Mira: "From the gallery, the dormitories, and two maintenance apprentices who heard Noctis officials talking near the west stair after the hearing."

Leon blinked.

Leon: "You organized informants?"

Mira's freckles darkened.

Mira: "Witnesses."

Professor Valen's eyes warmed.

Professor Valen: "An important distinction."

Mira crossed to me and held out the folio.

Haru: "You do not have to give that to me."

Mira: "I know."

Her hands trembled once before she steadied them.

Mira: "But you should know what people are willing to say before someone tells you they are too afraid."

I accepted the folio.

Inside were names, times, overheard phrases, descriptions of hallway pressure, and one page headed in Mira's careful handwriting:

**THINGS GREEN CIRCLE STUDENTS ARE TIRED OF BEING TOLD**

The first line read:

**That fear makes us unreliable.**

My chest tightened.

Mira saw it and looked away first, not out of shame, but mercy.

Mira: "You do not have to carry all of it."

Haru: "No."

I closed the folio carefully.

Haru: "But I should not pretend it weighs nothing."

Aira's pencil paused.

Only briefly.

Then she cleared her throat.

Aira: "Protocol review. Everyone listen, including the emotionally constipated and the politically overqualified."

Rhea: "Miss Solen."

Aira: "I said including."

Seraphine's mouth moved like she had decided not to enjoy that aloud.

Aira tapped the first sheet.

Aira: "Noctis wants three things. One: Cael returned. Two: their record tampering reframed as Aurelis interference. Three: Haru classified under pressure. We do not let them braid those together."

She tapped the second sheet.

Aira: "Our answer is sequence. What happened first. Who acted next. Who benefited from confusion. We keep returning to that."

Cassian: "And if Lucien makes it personal?"

Aira looked at me.

Aira: "He will."

The room did not soften around the answer.

Good.

Softening would have made it harder.

Elira stepped beside me.

Elira: "Then he wastes time proving what we already know."

Cael looked down at his bandaged wrist.

Cael: "He does not waste time."

His voice was quiet, but it made the room listen.

Cael: "If Lucien makes it personal, it is because he wants the trial to treat pain as evidence."

Professor Valen closed her folio.

Professor Valen: "Then we must keep asking whose pain."

Outside the room, the trial bell rang once.

Not academy silver.

Older.

Bronze and black glass.

The sound went through my sternum with a cold pressure and the faint taste of metal-ozone on my tongue.

Circle Zero had entered the morning.

Rhea turned toward the door.

President Valmont: "Witnesses to the hall."

No one joked after that.

---

The Foundation Witness Trial did not take place in the adjudication hall.

It began there.

Then the floor remembered something older.

As Rhea placed her council seal against the center table, the red wood split soundlessly along hidden seams. The witness sigils in the glass walls rose from their frames. The ceiling opened without exposing sky.

Below us, the east archive answered.

Silver water appeared in a circle beneath the floor, not flooding it, not reflecting it, but replacing the idea of depth with memory. Seven black steps unfolded downward from the center of the room.

Noctis officials objected before the first step finished forming.

Noctis Official: "Aurelis is altering the venue."

Rhea did not look at him.

President Valmont: "Aurelis is complying with the trial notice your relay accepted."

Professor Halden adjusted his glasses.

Professor Halden: "Technically, the venue is altering us."

Seraphine: "Unhelpful."

Professor Halden: "Precise."

The Noctis official's expression tightened.

At the guest entrance, Lucien Voss arrived in formal black.

He did not look like someone walking into enemy territory.

He looked like someone evaluating a room's weaknesses before deciding which one deserved to become fatal.

Nera stood two steps behind him, binding rings locked but dim.

Oric Sen stood beside her.

His shoulders were tight.

His eyes were open.

Soren Ashvale entered from the Valemere side in silver-blue formal uniform, scarred hands bare and visible. No weapon. No flourish. Honor stripped down to testimony.

Lucien glanced at him.

Lucien: "Valemere has become sentimental."

Soren: "No. Attentive."

Lucien smiled.

Lucien: "How similar those can look from below."

Elira's hand brushed my sleeve, stopping the response I had not realized my body wanted to make.

She was right.

Lucien did not need my anger.

He wanted my proof.

We descended.

The stairs led into a chamber that had not been under the tower yesterday.

Or perhaps it had always been there, waiting for the day modern walls became too thin for old arguments.

The Foundation Trial Hall was circular, cut from pale stone threaded with bronze and black glass. At the center, a wide ring of silver water lay flat as polished metal. Around it stood four witness platforms, each marked by a different kind of authority.

Council.

Faculty.

Field.

Institution.

Above them, colored circles floated in slow orbit: Green, Blue, Red, Yellow.

Behind those colors, so faint most people would call it shadow, a black ring moved in perfect silence.

My false Green Circle did not react.

I made sure of it.

The trial hall smelled like cold stone, rainwater, and candle smoke from a century no one had written honestly about.

Mira and the Green Circle witnesses filled the upper gallery with notebooks ready. They were not loud. That made them harder to dismiss.

One of the younger Green Circle students near Mira started to tuck her notebook beneath her sleeve when a Noctis official looked up.

Mira caught the edge of the page before it disappeared.

Not hard.

Just enough.

Mira: "Visible notes."

The younger student swallowed.

Green Student: "What if they mark our names?"

Mira's hand was shaking too. She did not hide it well enough to make courage look clean.

Mira: "Then they mark names attached to witnesses, not rumors."

Professor Valen, seated below at the faculty platform, heard her.

So did Professor Halden.

Valen did not turn around, but one corner of her mouth softened.

Halden did turn.

His gaze moved over the gallery, counting notebooks, hands, sightlines, exits, pressure points, and the Noctis official watching all of it with bureaucratic patience.

Professor Halden: "President Valmont."

Rhea looked back.

Professor Halden: "Gallery records should be mirrored under council seal before recess. Originals remain with their writers."

The Noctis official's expression tightened.

Rhea's did not.

President Valmont: "So ordered."

A red copy sigil unfolded above the gallery rail. Page reflections rose one by one, not stealing ink, only preserving shape.

The younger Green student stared at her own copied page floating in council light.

Green Student: "Can they do that?"

Mira's voice steadied.

Mira: "Apparently we can."

Across the hall, Aira looked up from her notes long enough to see the copy sigils catch.

Her pencil moved again, faster.

I had thought witnesses were people who stood beside you.

Now I was beginning to understand they were also people who refused to let the record be lonely.

Cael took the protected witness platform with Leon on his left and Cassian on his right. The healer seal around his wrist recognized the floor and flashed warning blue.

The hall answered in silver text.

**CONDUIT WITNESS PRESENT**

Cael's face tightened.

Rhea's voice cut through the chamber before Noctis could use the label.

President Valmont: "Record Cael Nox by name."

The text flickered.

**CAEL NOX: CONDUIT WITNESS**

Rhea: "Name first."

Another flicker.

**CAEL NOX**

Only then did Rhea sit.

Mira wrote so quickly her pencil scratched.

I understood why.

Sometimes resistance was not a speech.

Sometimes it was making the room say the name before the function.

The silver water brightened.

**FOUNDATION WITNESS TRIAL OPEN**

**QUESTION ONE: DID NOCTIS DOMINION COLLEGE CAUSE PROTECTED STUDENT HARM THROUGH COERCIVE CONDUIT USE?**

**QUESTION TWO: DID AURELIS ARCANE ACADEMY ALTER RECORDS TO CONCEAL UNAUTHORIZED CLASSIFICATION?**

**QUESTION THREE: MAY CLASSIFICATION BE COMPELLED BEFORE CAUSATION IS ESTABLISHED?**

The third question turned colder than the others.

Lucien looked at me.

Haru: "Sequence."

Aira, from behind the faculty witness table, whispered without looking up.

Aira: "Good boy."

I chose not to dignify that because dignity was apparently scarce today.

Rhea raised one hand.

President Valmont: "Phase One. Injury sequence."

The trial began.

---

Cael's testimony was the first blade laid on the table.

He stood because Noctis had always taught him to stand when questioned.

Then he sat again because Leon touched the back of his chair and said one word under his breath.

Leon: "Choice."

Cael looked startled by the reminder.

Then he stayed seated.

The room noticed.

Noctis noticed most.

Rhea's council seal recorded it before anyone could call it weakness.

President Valmont: "Cael Nox, do you understand that you may refuse any question that asks for private medical details beyond the scope of conduit injury?"

Cael: "Yes."

Noctis Official: "Noctis objects to restricting institutional inquiry."

Seraphine: "Noted."

Noctis Official: "That is not a ruling."

Seraphine: "I know."

Rhea: "Overruled."

The official sat back.

Cael gripped the arm of the chair with his uninjured hand.

President Valmont: "During Crown Field Team Combat, did you agree to serve as Lucien Voss' conduit under conditions that permitted refusal?"

The healer seal at Cael's wrist pulsed.

Cael: "No."

The word did not shake.

His fingers did.

President Valmont: "Did you understand refusal to be available to you?"

Cael looked at Lucien.

Lucien watched him with patient interest, as if Cael's courage were a minor variation in an experiment.

Cael looked away.

Cael: "No."

The silver water recorded the answer.

Noctis Official: "Student Nox was trained under Noctis formation discipline. He understood his role."

Cael's mouth tightened.

Cael: "I understood that if I failed the role, failure would become my identity."

The gallery went quiet in a different way.

Not shock.

Recognition traveling from person to person without asking permission.

For just a moment, Lucien's gaze lowered to Cael's injured wrist.

Something almost like regret passed behind his eyes.

Almost.

Then the Dominion in him closed around it. Regret was weakness, and weakness was death where he had been shaped. By the time he looked up again, only curiosity remained.

Mira stopped writing for half a breath.

Then she wrote that down too.

Rhea: "What happened when the conduit formed?"

Cael breathed in.

The sound came thin through his teeth.

Cael: "My channels locked open. I could feel Lucien's mana using mine as a path. The crown field pressure entered from outside, and his lightless mana entered from behind it. I could not close either direction."

Leon went very still beside him.

Cassian's fire did not rise.

That was how I knew he was furious.

Cael: "I tried to pull back."

Noctis Official: "Objection. Subjective interpretation."

Nera spoke before Rhea did.

Nera: "It is technically observable."

Every Noctis eye turned toward her.

Lucien's smile did not change.

Nera's face stayed pale and composed.

Nera: "Conduit withdrawal attempts leave a reverse-pressure mark at the wrist and sternum channels. Cael had both."

Halden's containment tag brightened.

Professor Halden: "Confirmed by Aurelis healer report."

Rhea: "Entered."

Cael looked at Nera like he had not expected the truth to arrive from that side of the room.

Nera did not look back.

Maybe she could not afford to.

Rhea: "Cael, what happened next?"

Cael swallowed once.

Cael: "I started to collapse."

The silver water showed the memory without asking him to relive all of it.

A crown field.

Lucien's lightless construct.

Elira's gold pressure cutting through it.

Cael's mana channels flaring too bright.

The seven missing seconds appeared as a dark gap.

The chamber leaned toward me.

Not physically.

Worse.

Conceptually.

Lucien's eyes sharpened.

Rhea spoke immediately.

President Valmont: "Pause record. The gap is not yet testimony. We are establishing injury sequence."

The dark gap stopped expanding.

I let out a breath I had not meant to hold.

Elira's fingers found mine beneath the witness table.

No one saw.

Or perhaps everyone did, and chose mercy.

Cael stared at the frozen memory.

Cael: "That is where Noctis wants the question to begin."

His voice grew steadier.

Cael: "But I was already breaking before the gap."

The trial hall rang once.

**SEQUENCE MARKED**

Lucien's expression remained pleasant.

That made the sound feel like a warning instead of a victory.

---

Soren Ashvale's testimony came next.

Valemere did not send a projection.

It sent a witness.

Soren stepped onto the neutral platform and placed one scarred hand over the silver rail. The old hall read him, recognized his Red Circle, and then did something I did not expect.

It dimmed the color.

**NEUTRAL WITNESS: SOREN ASHVALE**

No rank.

No family line.

No dueling record.

Just witness.

Soren saw it.

Respect moved across his face before he tucked it away.

Rhea: "You witnessed a prior match involving Lucien Voss and Talen Mire of Valemere?"

Soren: "Yes."

Lucien's gaze drifted toward him.

Lucien: "Careful, Ashvale. Memory becomes theatrical when honor edits it."

Soren did not look at him.

Soren: "Then I will be plain."

He faced the trial water.

Soren: "Talen Mire had already lost. The match bell had sounded. His shield was down. Lucien Voss maintained channel pressure for four seconds after the legal endpoint."

The water darkened.

Elira's hand tightened around mine.

I remembered her telling me about Talen in a quiet voice, remembered the way grief and anger had lived together under her composure.

Soren: "When the officials intervened, Lucien released him cleanly enough that the record called it delayed disengagement. Talen survived. His casting did not recover."

Lucien's voice was mild.

Lucien: "Valemere dislikes losing elegantly."

Soren turned at last.

Soren: "There was nothing elegant about a boy trying to move his fingers while adults argued over whether cruelty had met the definition of a foul."

The words struck harder because he did not raise his voice.

In the upper gallery, Mira's pencil stopped again.

Then every Green Circle student near her began writing.

Professor Valen closed her eyes.

Only briefly.

Elira's expression did not break.

But the gold light at her collarbone sharpened into a line thin enough to cut glass.

Rhea: "Did Elira Vale have romantic history with Lucien Voss relevant to this testimony?"

The question startled the room.

Then I understood.

Rhea was killing the rumor before Noctis could breed it.

Soren's answer was immediate.

Soren: "No."

Rhea: "Clarify."

Soren: "Lucien Voss was not Lady Vale's suitor, partner, rival in affection, or private scandal. He was a tournament cruelty she refused to excuse."

Elira looked at Rhea.

Rhea did not look back.

She was too busy protecting the record.

Soren: "If Noctis attempts to frame her opposition as personal romantic grievance, Valemere will testify that the grievance was ethical before Haru Aerlen ever arrived at Aurelis."

The trial hall recorded the statement in gold-white light.

Lucien's smile thinned by a fraction.

Small.

Precise.

Satisfying.

Noctis Official: "Prior match history does not establish present causation."

Professor Valen opened her eyes.

Professor Valen: "Pattern history establishes doctrine."

Professor Halden looked at her.

Professor Halden: "That is admissible under Foundation logic."

Professor Valen: "I know."

Soren stepped back.

As he passed my platform, he did not speak.

He only met my eyes and nodded once.

Not encouragement.

Reminder.

Let her keep choosing.

I looked at Elira.

She was already looking at me.

In a room built to force answers out of people, her silence gave me one.

---

Nera Vail looked smaller on the technical platform than she had ever looked beside Lucien.

Not weaker.

Just less armored by obedience.

Her binding rings rested flat against both wrists. Under the trial hall's silver light, the marks they had left on her skin were visible.

Oric stood behind the Noctis table with his hands clasped so tightly his knuckles had gone white.

Lucien watched Nera the way a person watches a bridge they suspect may collapse.

Rhea: "Nera Vail, you are listed as Noctis technical witness. Do you understand that false technical arrangement under Foundation trial carries institutional penalty?"

Nera: "Yes."

Rhea: "Do you understand that truthful testimony may conflict with Noctis delegation preference?"

The Noctis official stood.

Noctis Official: "Objection."

Rhea: "On what basis?"

Noctis Official: "Hostile phrasing."

Rhea: "Accurate phrasing is not hostility."

Seraphine: "Overruled."

Rhea looked back at Nera.

President Valmont: "Do you understand?"

Nera's throat moved.

Nera: "Yes."

Rhea: "Was Cael Nox's preparation standard by Noctis technical protocol?"

The room leaned in again.

Nera's fingers twitched near her rings.

Nera: "Yes."

The word landed like a crack in black glass.

Noctis Official: "Technical protocol differs by formation intensity."

Nera: "His preparation was standard for the formation submitted."

Noctis Official: "Submitted by whom?"

Nera did not look at Lucien.

That was the bravest thing she had done yet.

Nera: "Lucien Voss."

The silver water recorded the answer.

Lucien's smile remained.

His eyes did not.

Rhea: "Did the pressure applied during Crown Field Team Combat exceed the submitted formation?"

Nera's breathing changed.

Aira whispered, "Come on."

Nera: "Yes."

Oric closed his eyes.

He had heard her object in the calibration room.

Not loudly. Nera rarely wasted volume where precision would do. She had pointed to Cael's wrist tolerance, then to the crown-field pressure estimate, and said the numbers did not belong in the same formation.

Lucien had smiled.

Oric had looked at the slate, seen the same mismatch, and said nothing because silence was safer when the Dominion already knew what it wanted to hear.

Now Cael's injured wrist was in front of him.

So was Talen's name.

So was Nera, finally saying aloud what he had helped bury.

Nera continued before anyone could stop her.

Nera: "The submitted formation listed Cael as secondary relay support. In practice, he was used as a primary lightless conduit under crown field pressure. The wrist binders were calibrated for support tolerance, not forced channel opening."

Professor Halden's pen moved once.

Professor Halden: "Forced channel opening."

Nera flinched at the phrase, then steadied.

Nera: "Yes."

Noctis Official: "Miss Vail is interpreting under emotional influence."

Oric spoke from behind him.

Oric: "No."

The official turned slowly.

Oric looked like he wanted the floor to swallow him and had decided to testify before it could.

Oric: "I witnessed the calibration. Nera objected to the pressure range before the match. Lucien told her the conduit would either hold or prove useful by failing."

The trial hall went silent.

Lucien finally looked away from Nera.

Oric almost stepped back.

Then Cael stood.

Not fully.

Just enough that Leon moved with him and Cassian's hand hovered near the chair.

Cael looked at Oric.

Cael: "Thank you."

Oric's face folded around something too tired to be relief.

Nera lowered her head.

Not submission.

Grief.

Mira's gallery wrote every word.

Rhea allowed the silence to last long enough for the record to hold it.

Then Lucien spoke.

Lucien: "How touching."

He stepped forward before Noctis could stop him.

Lucien: "Aurelis has taught everyone a new trick. Call fear courage, call disobedience testimony, call weakness a person, and the room applauds itself for mercy."

Elira's gold crest brightened.

Lucien turned toward me.

Lucien: "But none of them have answered why a Green Circle transfer student stood at the center of every impossible correction."

There it was.

The trial shifted.

The air tasted like metal.

---

Lucien did not ask the question loudly.

He did not need to.

Lucien: "Haru Aerlen, what are you?"

The trial hall should have rejected it.

Rhea had ruled against classification pressure. The trial's own question had placed causation first. Every witness so far had dragged the record away from rank and back toward sequence.

But the Noctis Foundation Relay was listening through Lucien.

And Dominion doctrine had never respected a closed door just because someone had written law across it.

The colored circles above the trial water slowed.

Green.

Blue.

Red.

Yellow.

The faint black ring behind them became colder.

Not brighter.

Colder.

My sternum tightened.

Ozone and old metal touched the back of my tongue.

Elira's pendant answered beneath her uniform with a pulse only I could feel.

Noctis Official: "The question is relevant."

Rhea: "The question is prohibited."

Lucien: "By student council preference."

Professor Halden stood.

Professor Halden: "By trial sequence."

Lucien's gaze slid to him.

Lucien: "Professor Halden. Still starving your curiosity?"

Halden's face went pale with anger so controlled it became almost academic.

Professor Halden: "Feeding it properly."

He placed one containment tag on the edge of the silver water.

Professor Halden: "The record shows conduit harm began before the seven-second gap. Classification inquiry cannot precede established injury sequence."

Lucien: "Cannot, or should not?"

Professor Valen answered.

Professor Valen: "In old systems, those are sometimes the same word."

Lucien smiled at her.

Lucien: "History professors do love rooms that agree with them."

Professor Valen: "No. We fear them. That is why we read carefully."

The black ring behind the colors moved once.

I felt it look at me.

Not with eyes.

With function.

**FIELD WITNESS: ANSWER**

The words appeared above the water.

The room stilled.

Elira's hand found mine beneath the table again.

This time, I let people see it.

The Green Circle students saw.

Mira saw.

Lucien saw.

Good.

If they wanted the truth of my restraint, they could start with the fact that I was not standing alone.

I rose.

Haru: "During Crown Field Team Combat, I saw pressure forming around Cael Nox before the seven-second gap."

Lucien: "That is not what I asked."

Haru: "It is what happened."

The silver water brightened.

Haru: "Cael's channels were already under forced opening. Lucien's lightless construct used him as a path. Elira's strike broke the visible control. Leon and Cassian opened space. Aira corrected timing. I called the instability."

Noctis Official: "A Green Circle field reader cannot perceive all of that."

Every eye moved to my crest.

The false Green Circle rested harmlessly.

Small.

Convenient.

Haru: "Then your objection is about my rank, not Cael's injury."

Mira's pencil stopped.

Then moved faster.

Haru: "If your claim is that Cael was harmed by Aurelis interference, prove when the harm began. If your claim is that the records were altered, prove who benefited from the alteration. If your claim is that I am impossible, then you are asking the trial to study me instead of protecting him."

The chamber's silver water rippled.

Lucien's smile became very still.

Haru: "Classification does not decide causation."

The old hall rang.

**SEQUENCE ANCHOR ACCEPTED**

Something inside my chest loosened.

Not enough.

Never enough.

But enough to breathe.

Then the Noctis Foundation Relay answered.

Black glass unfolded above Lucien's witness platform in the shape of a narrow crown.

**DOMINION OBJECTION**

**MERCY MAY MASK POWER**

**POWER MUST BE NAMED TO BE GOVERNED**

The words struck the room like a law older than kindness.

Cael went white.

Nera's rings clicked shut.

Elira stood.

Gold light rose around her, controlled and furious.

Elira: "Power must be witnessed before it is governed."

The pendant beneath her collar darkened.

Lucien saw the fabric shift.

His eyes sharpened.

I felt the moment he understood that the necklace mattered.

The air around Elira turned dangerous.

Not because the pendant attacked.

Because it refused him.

Lucien's lightless pressure brushed toward it and stopped as if the space between them had become a wall.

The public saw nothing except Elira standing beside me with a Yellow Circle crest bright enough to make the old hall answer gold.

That was enough.

Elira: "Haru Aerlen is not your evidence. Cael Nox is not your property. Nera Vail is not your arranged truth. If Dominion cannot tell the difference between witness and possession, then Dominion is not fit to govern anything in this room."

For the first time all morning, Lucien's expression changed.

Not anger.

Interest refined into threat.

Lucien: "There you are, Elira."

Haru: "Do not."

My voice was quiet.

The trial hall heard it anyway.

The black ring behind the colors stopped moving.

Every old system in the room turned toward the space between my restraint and Lucien's smile.

Lucien: "How protective."

Haru: "No."

I looked at the silver water.

Haru: "How instructed."

The word landed strangely.

Lucien's eyes narrowed.

I continued before fear could become caution.

Haru: "The old Aurelis Record said safeguard doctrine failed when institutions isolated candidates and turned witnesses ignorant by design. Noctis is trying to repeat that failure inside a trial."

Professor Valen's breath caught softly.

Halden looked at me like I had handed him a key and a warning at the same time.

Rhea rose.

President Valmont: "Aurelis requests Phase One finding."

The silver water surged.

The Noctis crown pressed down.

For one heartbeat, the room became two histories trying to occupy one floor.

Then the Foundation Trial Hall answered.

**PHASE ONE FINDING**

**CONDUIT HARM PRECEDED RECORD GAP**

**NOCTIS PRESSURE ESTABLISHED**

**CLASSIFICATION INQUIRY DEFERRED**

The Green Circle gallery erupted.

Not cheering.

Writing.

Pages turned like rain.

Lucien looked up at the finding.

Then he laughed.

Softly.

Almost fondly.

Lucien: "Good."

No victory had ever sounded less comforting.

---

The Dominion Relay did not retreat.

It changed the battlefield.

The black crown above Lucien's platform split into three shards. Each shard showed a different reflection: Cael in the crown field, Nera at the calibration table, me standing with a harmless Green Circle crest while impossible pressure moved around a dying conduit.

Then the shards rearranged.

They formed a door.

Noctis Official: "Dominion requests Phase Two."

Rhea: "On what basis?"

Lucien answered instead.

Lucien: "Pattern history."

Soren's face hardened.

Elira's hand tightened around mine.

The silver water changed before Rhea could reject the request.

**PHASE TWO PETITION RECEIVED**

**PATTERN HISTORY: PRIOR CHANNEL HARM**

Professor Valen stood.

Professor Valen: "Noctis does not control Valemere records."

Lucien: "No. But pain remembers even when schools prefer not to."

That was when I realized he had not brought up Talen Mire to hide from the past.

He had brought us here because he wanted to use it.

Soren stepped forward.

Soren: "If you attempt to turn Talen into your defense, I will challenge every word."

Lucien's smile returned.

Lucien: "You will not need to."

The door of black glass opened.

Beyond it was not Noctis.

It was a small room lit by morning from another city, with a Valemere crest on the wall and a young man seated beside a table. His hair was brown, his shoulders narrow, and one hand rested in a support brace etched with water-healing script.

Elira stopped breathing.

Soren whispered one word.

Soren: "Talen."

The young man looked older than our age and younger than his eyes.

The trial hall identified him in silver.
